Each tab thickens toward its lower edge and then drops sharply onto the next course, whose upper edge has darker AO because it is overlapped. A 256×256 granule noise gives a coarse mineral-grain feel, with per-tab tone variation and slow wear layered on top. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the shingle proportions, colors, granules and wear are not measurements of any manufacturer or model, and nails, sealant strips, moss and natural aging are not modeled; no external artwork is cited or shipped. Defaults are a 1.0 m nominal tile, 0.0045 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger to resolve the slots and granules; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`lawnstripes` adds mown lawn-stripe schematics, bringing the current catalog from 895 to 896 kinds (897 gallery choices including plain). Each tile has 8 horizontal mowing stripes alternating bright and dark (±0.14), with stripe borders wobbled by periodic noise. Within a stripe, blade strands come from two layers of value noise stretched along v (192×48 and 384×96) sampled through the integer shear x = u + lean·v (lean = 1, +1 on even stripes, −1 on odd), so the blades lean opposite ways in adjacent stripes while both axes stay periodic. Slow clump tone, strand-valley AO and slight roughness variation are added. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: individual blade geometry, dead grass, bare soil and weeds are not modeled; the lean is fixed at 45° (a limit of the integer shear), not a measurement of real grass; no external artwork is cited or shipped. Defaults are a 2.0 m nominal tile, 0.0028 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger to resolve the strands; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`atomicboomerang` adds atomic-age boomerang laminate schematics, bringing the current catalog from 896 to 897 kinds (898 gallery choices including plain). Each of the 5×5 cells carries one print: about 62% are V-shaped boomerangs (arms y = k|x| with k = 0.4 and width tapering to the tips as 1−t³), and about 70% of the remaining cells hold a four-point astroid star (|x|^0.55 + |y|^0.55 ≤ r^0.55). Every print has a random position, scale (80–100%) and angle, and is either pale cream or deep teal. The ground is pale gray-teal with fine dark speckle (a 160×160 noise threshold); prints are slightly raised and glossier. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored retro-pattern schematic, not a copy of any laminate brand's or model's design; no external artwork is cited or shipped. The print's center offset plus its half length stay below half a cell, so every print lies entirely inside its own cell and a point only needs its own cell. Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.0008 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger to resolve the star and boomerang tips; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`celticplait` adds carved Celtic two-ribbon plait schematics, bringing the current catalog from 897 to 898 kinds (899 gallery choices including plain). Four horizontal carved bands each consist of two interlacing ribbons (y = ±A·sin(2π·4u), A = 0.052, ribbon half-width 0.029), giving 8 crossings per tile; at every crossing the ribbon that is rising passes over the falling one, so each ribbon alternates over and under along its length. Ribbons have a round cross-section height, a thin center groove and a contact shadow (AO only) on whatever lies beneath; two thin rails separate the bands, and the recessed stone ground carries fine grain. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored, simplified two-strand plait: the three-or-more-strand structures, corners and zoomorphic forms of Celtic knotwork are not modeled; no external artwork is cited or shipped. The perpendicular distance to each ribbon is approximated by the vertical offset divided by √(1+slope²), an approximation rather than an exact offset curve. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.0042 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger to resolve the center groove; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`garnetschist` adds garnet mica-schist schematics, bringing the current catalog from 898 to 899 kinds (900 gallery choices including plain). The base is wavy foliation stretched along u: periodic noise wobbles v, two value-noise layers (3×28 and 6×112 cells) add coarse and fine layering, and sparse mica flecks come from a 224² noise threshold. About 60% of the 5×5 cells hold one garnet porphyroblast with a hexagonal outline (distance is the largest of three face-normal projections, apothem 0.1–0.19 of a cell), six pyramid faces with their own tones, a brighter crown at the center, raised, smoother and lower in AO, flanked by a pale quartz pressure shadow stretched along the foliation (major axis 2 radii, minor axis 0.7 radii). The dark ramp endpoint is dark red-brown, so garnets read dark red-brown and the schist pinkish brown. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the garnet is a 2D hexagon with six facets, not a true dodecahedral or trapezohedral crystal habit; the foliation does not actually bend around the porphyroblasts, and the directional glint of mica is not modeled. One scalar ramp only approximates red and is not spectral or mineralogical measurement. No external artwork is cited or shipped. Each garnet's circumscribed circle and pressure shadow plus position jitter stay below half a cell, so a point only queries its own cell. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.0035 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger to resolve the hexagonal outlines; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`topographiccontours` adds topographic contour-map schematics, bringing the current catalog from 899 to 900 kinds (901 gallery choices including plain). Three periodic value-noise layers (3², 6² and 12² cells, weights 0.5/0.3/0.2) form a smooth elevation field f, divided into 16 contour intervals per tile on pale beige paper with hypsometric tinting (each interval changes the base tone by 0.42/16). Contours use |f − nearest level| ÷ |∇f| (the gradient from forward differences) as spatial distance, so line width does not vary with slope; every 5th line is a thicker, darker index contour. Subtle paper-fiber noise is added. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: it is not real terrain data and has no elevation labels, rivers, coordinate grid or legend; the gradient has a floor so flat areas do not blow up the line width, and very close contours (steep slopes) merge because of their line width. Defaults are a 1.0 m nominal tile, 0.0012 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Each pixel needs three samples of the elevation field (three noise layers each); prefer 512 or larger for the fine lines, 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`digitalcamo` adds digital pixel-camouflage schematics, bringing the current catalog from 900 to 901 kinds (902 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 64×64 square pixels, each with one of four tones (dark olive, olive brown, khaki, sand). The tone comes from two layers of periodic value noise (4² and 12²) plus a per-block hash jitter, and about half of the pixels borrow the value of their 2×2 parent block (32²), clustering the pixels into larger squares. The quartiles of all pixel values are the thresholds, so each tone covers exactly a quarter of the pixels. A 128×128-thread-per-tile plain-weave canvas lies over the print (tone ±0.03, with height and AO variation). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic, not a copy of any military or commercial camouflage pattern, and it makes no claim about visible or near-infrared concealment; no external artwork is cited or shipped. Defaults are a 1.0 m nominal tile, 0.0007 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Building a sampler sorts 4096 pixel values (millisecond scale); prefer 512 or larger so each pixel spans at least 8 px and the weave shows, 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`radarscope` adds radar-scope display schematics, bringing the current catalog from 901 to 902 kinds (903 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one circular scope (radius 0.46 UV) on a dark green-black ground: 5 evenly spaced range rings (the outer one heavier), 24 bearing spokes every 15° (every 6th a heavier crosshair), one bright sweep arm at a seed-chosen angle, a 1.3 rad phosphor afterglow behind it that fades quadratically, and 6 blips at random polar positions (brighter the closer they are behind the arm, keeping at least 40% brightness). 192 horizontal scanlines modulate brightness, with slight noise. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored UI-style schematic: it is not the screen of any radar model and has no text, scale numbers or real range/bearing meaning; the sweep arm is fixed in the tile and does not move over time. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.0004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Each pixel loops over at most 6 blips; prefer 512 or larger to resolve the thin lines and blips, 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`hazardstripes` adds worn hazard-warning-stripe schematics, bringing the current catalog from 902 to 903 kinds (904 gallery choices including plain). Yellow-and-black stripes run at 45° (the integer shear a = u + v, 8 stripes per tile, i.e. 4 yellow and 4 black) over steel; the paint chips away according to three wear terms: 96² noise chips, scratches stretched along the stripes (192×10-cell noise in the integer-shear coordinates a = u + v and b = u − v) and an edge weight favoring stripe borders (+0.35 within 0.3 of a stripe width of a border). Where the paint is gone, bare steel shows through as the neutral gray (153,153,139) of the existing mortar mix, while the metallic M mask and the mortar weight are the same wear value (M1 = bare steel, M0 = paint). Paint keeps pure yellow / pure black base tones; roughness is 0.45 yellow, 0.62 black and 0.36 steel; paint stands 0.14 above the steel and chipped edges have slightly darker AO.

This is an authored schematic: it is not the color or stripe proportion mandated by any standard (for example OSHA or ISO 3864) and has no rust, welds, rivets or dents; bare steel is only approximated by the existing neutral gray, not an alloy or optical measurement. No external artwork is cited or shipped. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.0032 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 1; explicit `createMaterial({ metallic: 0 })` changes only the scalar factor, not the stored M mask. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger to resolve chips and scratches; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`kiwislices` adds kiwi-fruit slice schematics, bringing the current catalog from 903 to 904 kinds (905 gallery choices including plain). Each of the 2×2 cells holds one round slice (radius 0.42 of a cell, with jittered position, 92–100% scale and starting angle): green flesh with 64 pale radial fibers (darker away from the core), a pale yellow-green core disc (radius 0.115 of a cell), two rings of dark oval seeds outside the core (20 on the inner ring at 0.175 of a cell, 28 on the outer ring at 0.24, offset by half a pitch), and a dark-brown skin ring with fine fuzz noise (width 0.045 of a cell); dark gaps separate the fruit. Seeds are recessed with darker AO and the core is slightly lower; flesh is smooth (roughness 0.24) and skin rough (0.9). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic, not a measurement of any cultivar or cut: seed counts and positions, fiber count and colors are simplified, and no external artwork is cited or shipped. Each slice's radius plus its position jitter stays below half a cell, so a point only queries its own cell. Defaults are a 0.25 m nominal tile, 0.0018 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger to resolve seeds and fibers; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`mandalaflower` adds mandala-flower schematics, bringing the current catalog from 904 to 905 kinds (906 gallery choices including plain). One flower is centered on the tile center (the seed chooses its overall rotation). From the outside in: 12 large lotus petals (polar rose edge ρ = 0.30 + 0.15·|cos(6θ)|^0.8), a ring of 36 dots (radius 0.27), 24 outer petals (ρ = 0.12 + 0.11·|cos(12θ)|^1.5), 12 inner petals (phase-shifted by half a petal pitch) and a central hub (radius 0.035). Every layer is a filled region with a dark outline (width 0.008) painted back to front; the ground is recessed and later layers are higher. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored simplified schematic, not a mandala pattern of any religion, tradition or culture; no external artwork is cited or shipped. The outline width is the radial difference between the fill boundary ρ and boundary − width, not a distance perpendicular to the boundary. Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.0014 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Each pixel needs two trigonometric calls (atan2 and the rose-edge cosines); prefer 512 or larger to resolve the inner petals and the dot ring, 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`needlepoint` adds needlepoint-tapestry schematics, bringing the current catalog from 905 to 906 kinds (907 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 48×48 half-cross stitches slanted along (1, 1) (half-width 0.27 and half-length 0.66 of a cell, tapering at the ends with a fourth-power profile, plus a two-ply twist light/dark ripple), with dark canvas gaps showing between stitches. Color comes from a 12×12-stitch motif repeated 4×4 per tile: concentric diamond rings (ring width 2 stitches, a three-tone cycle) with a bright cross accent through the motif center, and every stitch has ±0.03 tone jitter. The motif is mirror-symmetric in both x and y. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored simplified schematic: it is not a measurement of a particular stitch (such as continental or basketweave) or design, and wool fuzz, canvas weave detail and the back of the work are not modeled. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.0026 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so each stitch spans at least 10 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`dicefaces` adds dice-face schematics, bringing the current catalog from 906 to 907 kinds (908 gallery choices including plain). A 4×4 field of rounded-square ivory faces (half edge 0.43 of a cell, corner radius 0.11, with a domed bevel at the face edge and dark gaps between faces), each hash-chosen to carry 1–6 recessed pips. The pips sit on a 3×3 lattice (spacing 0.255 of a cell, radius 0.078) in the standard point-symmetric layouts: 1 = center; 2 = diagonal; 3 = diagonal plus center; 4 = corners; 5 = corners plus center; 6 = two rows of three; each face is also randomly turned by 90° and mirrored so the 2, 3 and 6 layouts vary in direction. Pips are hemispherical recesses (depth 0.35, darker AO); faces are glossy (roughness 0.3) and gaps rough (0.9). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic, not the specification of any dice maker: opposite faces summing to 7 and left- or right-handed numbering belong to a whole die, not a single-face texture, and are not modeled. No external artwork is cited or shipped. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.0034 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger to resolve the pip edges; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`cinderblockwall` adds a concrete-masonry-unit wall, bringing the current catalog from 907 to 908 kinds (909 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 8 courses × 4 blocks in a half-block running bond (odd courses shifted right), blocks at a 2:1 aspect; recessed mortar joints 0.0095 tile wide use the existing `mortar` channel with the neutral grout color. Block faces carry a ±0.08 per-block tone shift, a low-frequency cloudy mottle, 160×160 aggregate grain relief, chamfered edges and about 940 small air voids per tile (one per 56×56 cell with probability 0.3, radius 0.0025–0.0059 tile) that are recessed and darker in color and AO. Metallic is 0.

This is an authored schematic, not a measurement of a particular CMU specification (such as ASTM C90 dimensions, cores and face shells): hollow cores, squeezed-out mortar, efflorescence and paint are not modeled. Defaults are a 1.6 m nominal tile, 0.0038 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger to resolve the voids; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`moirefringes` adds moire fringes, bringing the current catalog from 908 to 909 kinds (910 gallery choices including plain). Two transparent fine-line gratings are overlaid: integer wave vectors (24, 3) and (24, −3) (24 near-vertical lines per tile with opposite small tilts), a line fraction of 0.26, and light passes only where neither sheet has ink. The gratings' difference (0, 6) forms 6 horizontal moire bands per tile (wide and light where lines coincide, dense and dark where they interleave), and their sum (48, 0) is a second, finer set of fringes; the paper also carries a low-amplitude 256×256 fiber variation in color and height. Ink lines are slightly raised and smoother; metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored optical schematic, not a physical moire simulation: there is no observer view, camera sampling or screen sub-pixels; the fringes are the mathematical product of the two gratings. Because the wave vectors are integers, both gratings and their difference and sum are continuous across the tile boundary. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.0014 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so each line spans roughly 5 px or more; at very low resolutions or strong minification the texture itself produces extra aliasing moire. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`azulejotiles` adds Portuguese-style painted ceramic tiles, bringing the current catalog from 909 to 910 kinds (911 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds a 4×4 grid of identical glazed square tiles separated by recessed grout lines 0.012 tile wide (using the existing `mortar` channel with the neutral grout color); each tile carries a cobalt-on-white glaze design with full square symmetry: a four-lobed central rosette (radius 0.15 of a tile, lobe amplitude 0.055), a thin ring at radius 0.3, and quarter-disc corner motifs that join into full circles where four tiles meet (with a white thin ring inside). The blue has brush-stroke tone variation, the glaze carries faint craquelure lines on a 28×28 cell scale per tile, tile edges are bevelled, the glaze is smooth (roughness 0.2) and the grout rough. Metallic is 0.

This is an authored schematic, not a measurement of a particular historical design or origin (such as Delft or Talavera); there is no hand-painted variation between tiles — every tile is identical, and because the design has full square symmetry, turning a tile would make no visible difference in this material. Defaults are a 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.0022 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`tumblingblocks` adds a tumbling-blocks (isometric cube) patchwork, bringing the current catalog from 910 to 911 kinds (912 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds a hexagonal lattice of 7 columns × 8 rows; every pointy-top hexagon (circumradius 1) is split by three rays from its center to the upper-right, upper-left and bottom vertices into a top face (light, tone 0.92), a right face (mid, 0.62) and a left face (dark, 0.3), giving the isometric-cube illusion. Each face also gets ±0.06 per-block tone jitter, fine woven-fabric grain, and thin seams between faces (dark, slightly recessed, darker AO). The lattice is aligned with a hexagon center at u = v = 0; because the UV tile is square and the lattice is 7√3 wide by 12 tall (about 1.01), the hexagons are stretched about 1% non-uniformly. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored patchwork schematic, not a measurement of a particular quilt design: there is no stitching, batting or fabric wrinkling, and the cubes' 3D appearance is baked tone (the light and dark faces do not respond to light direction). Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.0018 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`kilimflatweave` adds a kilim flatweave rug, bringing the current catalog from 911 to 912 kinds (913 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 48×48 weave cells arranged top to bottom in four 12-row bands: stepped diamonds (12-cell period, mirror-symmetric in both axes, stepped concentric colors), a stepped zigzag (6-cell period, each row half in each of two colors), horizontal stripes (a 12-row palindrome: dark, dark, red, red, ochre, white, white, ochre, red, red, dark, dark) and crosses (44 of the 144 cells in each 12×12 motif carry the cross color). Colors are four tone steps (indigo, madder red, saffron, undyed-wool white) with ±0.025 per-cell dye jitter, horizontal weft-faced ribs (3 per cell) and a low-amplitude fibre mottle; where the color changes between neighboring cells the cell's left edge carries a 0.14-cell-wide slit (dark, recessed, darker AO), imitating the vertical slits of a flatweave at color boundaries. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored simplified schematic, not a measurement of a particular tribe or region's kilim, and no traditional provenance is claimed; warp/weft interlacing detail, fringes and wear are not modeled. The four tone steps are brightness rather than hue (colors interpolate linearly between the dark and light ends). Defaults are a 1.2 m nominal tile, 0.0022 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so each cell spans about 10 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`ean13barcodes` adds EAN-13 barcode labels, bringing the current catalog from 912 to 913 kinds (914 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 4 labels stacked vertically, each filling the tile width (9 modules of quiet zone on each side plus 95 modules of bars, 113 modules in all), encoded per the EAN-13 specification: start guard 101, six left digits (L or G codes chosen by the first digit's parity pattern), center guard 01010, six right digits (R codes), end guard 101, and a correct thirteenth check digit. The twelve data digits derive from the seed and row (so rows differ); they are not registered product numbers and no human-readable digits are drawn. Guard bars run slightly lower than digit bars, bar edges are softened, the ink has low-amplitude density variation and the paper faint fibers. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the symbols are generated from the published EAN-13 encoding tables so that structural "scanning" is valid, but print quality (such as ISO 15416 grades), bar-width gain compensation, human-readable text and the leading digit are not modeled; the tests verify structure and check digits only and no physical scanner or decoder library was used. Defaults are a 0.35 m nominal tile, 0.0012 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so each module spans about 9 px; at 512 a module is about 4.5 px and edge softening takes a large share of it; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`morsetape` adds Morse-code paper tape, bringing the current catalog from 913 to 914 kinds (915 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 10 tape lines, each 64 time units wide; every line spells one word chosen by hash from ten (SOS, XYZ, CAT, PLAY, HELLO, WORLD, LIGHT, ENGINE, SIGNAL, TEXTURE) in International Morse timing: dot = 1 unit, dash = 3 units, 1 unit between marks of a letter, 3 units between letters, 3 units of blank margin at the left and blank tape for the remainder so the pattern is not cut at the tile boundary. Marks are round-ended capsules one unit thick; each line has a faint center guide, the ink has low-amplitude density variation and the paper faint fibers. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic, not a measurement of a particular telegraph tape standard: sprocket holes along the tape, ink wobble and fading are not modeled; each line holds a single word (so the 7-unit word gap never appears) and the vocabulary is a fixed ten words. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.0016 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so each unit spans about 8 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`sandripples` adds wind-formed sand ripples, bringing the current catalog from 914 to 915 kinds (916 gallery choices including plain). A train of ripples on the integer wave vector (18, 7), about 18 crests crossing the u direction per tile; the crests are bent by two periodic low-frequency noise fields (6×6 and 24×24 cells) and the profile is asymmetric: the windward face takes 72% of the phase and rises gently, the lee face takes 28% and drops steeply, with crest and trough rounded by a smooth function. The windward side is lighter, the lee side darker with extra AO; there is also 384×384 grain relief and bright mica/mineral specks in about 5% of cells. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored simplified schematic, not a measurement of a particular dune: crest bifurcations (defects), dune-scale forms, wind-direction changes and wet sand are not modeled. Defaults are a 1 m nominal tile, 0.006 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`frostpolygons` adds arctic patterned ground, bringing the current catalog from 915 to 916 kinds (917 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 8×8 jittered Voronoi polygons (jitter 0.7): silt polygon centers (slightly lower, mid-tone with low-frequency cloudy variation), a raised stony rim around each (darker, with round pebbles of random radius and tone 0.3–0.7), and narrow, deep, dark ice-wedge troughs (darker AO) between polygons. Polygon borders are approximated from the gap between the two nearest seed distances, not a true edge distance, so trough width varies slightly at sharp corners. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored simplified schematic, not a measurement of a particular permafrost site (such as tundra polygons or stone circles): ice-wedge profiles, freeze–thaw cycles, moss and seasonal snow are not modeled, and the rim stones vary in brightness only, with no lithology. Defaults are a 1.5 m nominal tile, 0.012 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`datamatrixcodes` adds Data Matrix 2D-code labels, bringing the current catalog from 916 to 917 kinds (918 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 3×3 ECC 200-style symbols of 18×18 modules (each with a 1-module quiet zone around it, 60×60 modules in all): the left column and bottom row form the solid L-shaped finder, the top row and right column the alternating clock track (the top row is dark on even columns and the right column is dark on even steps up from the bottom, so the top-right corner is light), and each module of the interior 16×16 data region is inked with about 50% probability from the seed. Modules are square with edges softened only against a neighbor of the other state, so runs of dark modules stay solid; the ink has low-amplitude density variation and the paper faint fibers. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the finder and clock track follow the Data Matrix structure, but the data region holds random bits, not ECC 200 codewords (no payload, Reed–Solomon error correction or codeword placement), so the symbols cannot be scanned and no scanner or decoder library was used; the tests verify only the finder, clock track, quiet zone and rough data density. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.0012 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so each module spans about 17 px (about 8.5 px at 512); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`ecgpaper` adds ECG chart paper, bringing the current catalog from 917 to 918 kinds (919 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is 80 mm square of pink recording paper: a 1 mm minor grid and heavier lines every 5 mm (paper tone 0.96, minor lines 0.84, major lines 0.68) plus 4 ECG trace rows, each 20 mm tall with 4 heartbeats (20 mm each, about 0.8 s or 75 bpm at 25 mm/s). Every beat is a sum of Gaussian P, Q, R, S and T waves (P +0.9 mm, Q −0.8 mm, R +7 mm, S −1.8 mm, T +1.7 mm) with ±6% amplitude jitter per beat and a 0.25 mm baseline drift of one period per tile. The trace is a dark ink line with half width 0.2 mm, rendered by the first-order distance (vertical offset divided by √(1 + slope²)); at very steep spikes such as the R wave the inked line therefore grows much thicker vertically than 0.2 mm (measured: the R-wave ink reaches about 8.3 mm above baseline against the 7 mm authored amplitude). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic, not a recording or diagnostic data of any real ECG: lead labels, calibration pulses, heart-rate variability, noise, pathological waveforms and the 12-lead layout are not modeled. Defaults are a 0.08 m nominal tile (80 mm square, matching the paper's real millimetres), 0.0012 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so a 1 mm cell spans about 12.8 px (about 6.4 px at 512); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`sheetmusic` adds sheet music, bringing the current catalog from 918 to 919 kinds (920 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 8 five-line staves (row pitch 0.125 tile, line spacing 1/64 tile), 16 beats per staff with a barline every 4 beats, and one note per beat whose pitch is hash-chosen from the 9 half-spacing positions within the staff (bottom line to top line, no ledger lines). Noteheads are tilted ellipses, about 30% hollow (half-note style) and the rest filled (quarter-note style). Stems follow the notation rule: notes below the middle line (position < 4) have stems up on the right of the head; notes on or above the middle line have stems down on the left, 3.5 spacings long. The ink has low-amplitude density variation and the paper faint fibers. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic, not any piece of music: clefs, time and key signatures, flags, beams, rests, duration-proportional spacing and lyrics are not modeled, and a hollow note does not change the beat count (every note still occupies one beat slot), so it is not playable. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.0014 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so a staff spacing spans about 16 px; at 512 it is about 8 px and the stems, only about 0.8 px wide, become faint; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`sudokugrid` adds a Sudoku grid, bringing the current catalog from 919 to 920 kinds (921 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one 9×9 Sudoku board with thin lines between cells (half width 0.018 cell) and thick lines between the 3×3 boxes (half width 0.04 cell). Each cell shows a clue digit with about 42% probability from the seed, drawn in a 3×5 pixel font (0.135 cell per pixel). The clues come from a hidden complete solution built from the canonical pattern with digit relabelling and band, in-band row and in-band column permutations, all of which preserve the Sudoku rules, so no row, column or box contains a repeated clue. The ink has low-amplitude density variation and the paper faint fibers. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the clues are a random subset of the full solution and do not guarantee a unique solution or any difficulty grade, and the font is a 3×5 pixel font rather than real print type. Defaults are a 0.25 m nominal tile, 0.0014 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so each cell spans about 57 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`measuringtape` adds a measuring tape, bringing the current catalog from 920 to 921 kinds (922 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is 160 mm square (16 cm wide) holding 8 yellow tape strips (20 mm tall each): a short tick (4 mm long) every 1 mm, a medium tick (6.4 mm) every 5 mm and a long, slightly thicker tick (9.2 mm) every 10 mm, with dark edge lines along the top and bottom of each strip; right of each centimeter tick, the centimeter number 0–15 is written in a 3×5 pixel font (1.1 mm per pixel; two digits sit side by side). The ink has low-amplitude density variation and the tape faint grain. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: it is a repeating 0–15 cm scale, not a measurement of any tape standard (such as a metric accuracy class); inch scales, the curvature of a metal tape, font weight and wear are not modeled. The labels are fixed to this segment's centimeter indices, so 15 meets 0 at the tile boundary. Defaults are a 0.16 m nominal tile (matching the real 160 mm), 0.0018 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so 1 mm spans about 6.4 px; at 512 a millimeter is only about 3.2 px and the ticks become very fine; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`calendarpage` adds a calendar page, bringing the current catalog from 921 to 922 kinds (923 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one page of 7 columns × 6 rows of month cells, each with its date in the top-left corner in a 3×5 pixel font, thin lines between cells, a heavier frame at the tile edge, and slightly darker paper in the first and last columns (the weekend). Every seed picks a month: the starting column (0–6) and the number of days (28–31); the day numbers run 1, 2, … in reading order to the month end, and the remaining cells stay blank. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: there is no weekday header row, month name, year, holiday or handwritten note, and the month does not correspond to any real one — it is an arbitrary start column and length; cells hold only the date number. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.0014 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so each cell spans about 73 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`fretboard` adds a guitar fretboard, bringing the current catalog from 922 to 923 kinds (924 gallery choices including plain). Each tile covers one octave (from the nut to the 12th fret) of dark rosewood: fret positions follow twelve-tone equal temperament, fret n sitting at 1 − 2^(−n/12) of the scale length, normalized by the 12th fret (half the scale) onto u = 1 so it meets the next tile's nut at u = 0; each fret gap is 2^(−1/12) ≈ 0.944 of the previous one. Six strings run along u, evenly spaced across the width, with half widths growing from 0.0022 (treble side) to 0.0075 (bass side) and a faint shadow beside each; pearl dots sit at the midpoints of frets 3, 5, 7 and 9, and a symmetric double dot at the midpoint of the 12th fret. The wood grain is stretched along u. Metallic is 0 (frets and strings use brightness and roughness only, not the metal mask).

This is an authored schematic, not a measurement of a particular instrument: the scale length is normalized, so only the fret-spacing ratios exist and no real millimetre sizes; fretboard radius, neck taper, string dents and wear are not modeled, and frets and strings are not metallic reflectors (they are brighter, smoother non-metals). Defaults are a 0.32 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so a fret is about 3.6 px wide (about 1.8 px at 512); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`crosswordgrid` adds a crossword grid, bringing the current catalog from 923 to 924 kinds (925 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one 15×15 crossword grid: about 17% of the cells are black, chosen from the seed by repeatedly adding black cells in 180°-rotationally symmetric pairs and accepting a pair only if every across and down run of white cells stays at least 3 cells long and all white cells stay connected. White cells that start an across or down word carry the standard number in reading order (1, 2, …, drawn in the cell's top-left corner in a 3×5 pixel font); the other white cells stay blank, black cells are ink-black, and thin lines separate the cells. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the cells are an empty crossword grid with no answer letters, clue text or heavy outer frame, and no word list guarantees the grid can be filled; the rules and numbering hold only inside a single tile — when the tile repeats, white runs at its edge continue across the tile boundary, so the repeated surface is not one valid large crossword. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.0014 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so a cell spans about 68 px (number pixels about 3.4 px); at 512 a cell is about 34 px and the numbers get very small; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`flipdotdisplay` adds a flip-dot display, bringing the current catalog from 924 to 925 kinds (926 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 48×48 discs on a black panel, each of radius 0.42 cell: lit discs are yellow (tone 0.92, glossier, slightly raised, with an upper-left highlight and a centre pivot mark) and unlit discs are dark gray-black (0.14). The whole panel is a 12-column × 8-row grid of digits, each digit taking 4×6 dots, lit as a 3×5 pixel glyph with one unlit dot of margin to the right and below; the digit values (0–9) come from the seed. The panel carries faint grain and darker AO between discs. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the flip animation, the magnetic bistable mechanism, the thickness of a disc's two faces (black/yellow) and mechanical clearances are not modeled; the digits are a random sequence with no clock, station name or other meaning. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.003 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so a disc spans about 21 px (about 11 px at 512, where the highlight and pivot become tiny); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`minesweeperboard` adds a Minesweeper board, bringing the current catalog from 925 to 926 kinds (927 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one 24×24 board: every cell hides a mine with 16% probability from the seed (the mines themselves are not drawn), adjacent-mine counts are computed on a torus so the tile edges join without a seam, and the board is opened by flood-fill from 7 safe zero cells (a zero cell opens all 8 neighbors). Opened cells are flat, darker and carry a thin grid line, with a digit 1–8 in a 3×5 pixel font when any neighbor is a mine; unopened cells are raised buttons with a light bevel at the top-left and a dark one at the bottom-right. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: there are no flags, question marks, exploded mines or digit colors (tones are brightness, not hue); the mines under the unopened area are fixed but invisible, so the picture only guarantees consistency of necessary conditions (see ACCEPTANCE) and not unique solvability. Because adjacency wraps at the tile boundary, this is not a standard bounded Minesweeper board. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.003 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so each cell spans about 43 px (about 21 px at 512); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`tetrisstack` adds a Tetris-style stack, bringing the current catalog from 926 to 927 kinds (928 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one 16×16 well: from the seed it repeatedly tries to place one of the seven tetrominoes (I, O, T, S, Z, J, L) in a random rotation and position, accepting a placement only if it lies fully inside the well and overlaps nothing, so every piece has exactly 4 cells; each of the seven kinds has its own tone (seven brightness steps from 0.9 to 0.2), cells have bevels and a highlight, pieces are separated by dark outlines (two touching pieces of the same kind are also split by an outline) and unoccupied cells are near-black pits. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic, not a real game position: there is no gravity, line clearing, falling piece or game rule, and nothing guarantees the stack could arise in play — it is a random non-overlapping placement; the tones are brightness, not the game's standard hues. Pieces at the tile edge do not cross the boundary, so repeated tiles simply sit side by side. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.0035 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so a cell spans about 32 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`battleshipgrid` adds a Battleship board, bringing the current catalog from 927 to 928 kinds (929 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one 10×10 board: blue-gray water with thin grid lines and a fleet of ships of 5, 4, 3, 3 and 2 cells, every ship a straight dark hull (adjacent cells merge into one rounded plank, inset where it meets water) with no two ships touching, even at corners; each cell is shot with about 32% probability from the seed: a shot on a ship leaves a dark peg (hit), a shot on water a near-white peg (miss). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: there are no coordinate labels, sunk-ship markings or ship-class shapes, and the shot record is random rather than turn-based, so it is not guaranteed to occur in a real game; the ship positions are fixed per seed. When the tile repeats, each board is an independent game and ships do not cross the boundary. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.003 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so a cell spans about 51 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`hilbertcurve` adds a Hilbert curve, bringing the current catalog from 928 to 929 kinds (930 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one order-4 Hilbert space-filling curve through the centers of 16×16 = 256 cells, produced by the standard index-to-coordinate algorithm: it starts in the top-left cell and ends in the top-right cell, cells with consecutive indices are joined by line segments and no other neighboring cells are joined. The line is 0.28 cell wide with a tone ramp along the curve index from deep indigo (0.08) to gray (0.55), on paper with faint fibers and low-amplitude ink density variation. The first and last cells each extend a half-cell stub beyond the tile edge, so when the tile repeats horizontally the previous tile's end joins the next tile's start into one continuous curve; vertically adjacent tiles are not connected. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the tone jumps from lightest back to darkest at the tile seam (a tone discontinuity between index 255 and 0), which is deliberate; no marker labels the start and end, and no other order or space-filling curve is offered. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.0014 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so a cell spans about 32 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`mondriancomposition` adds a Mondrian-style composition, bringing the current catalog from 929 to 930 kinds (931 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds a rectangle composition from recursive binary partitioning: the first two levels always split, after which each rectangle keeps splitting with 80% probability (at most 5 levels deep, minimum side 0.14 tile); each split line spans only the rectangle it divides, as in the paintings, with a split ratio between 35% and 65%; the tile edge also carries a black frame, so the composition is closed and continuous when repeated. Black lines have a half width of 0.0075 tile, each rectangle is filled with one of six tones (0.97 three times, 0.62, 0.4, 0.78, so about half the rectangles are white), the canvas has faint grain and the colored rectangles add brush variation. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: it is not a copy of any Mondrian work and has no red, blue and yellow — the fills are brightness only (colors interpolate linearly between the dark and light ends, so the result is grayscale); the partition rules and ratios are the author's. Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.0016 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so a line is about 3.8 px wide; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`lissajousgrid` adds a Lissajous-figure table, bringing the current catalog from 930 to 931 kinds (932 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds a 4×4 oscilloscope-style table of Lissajous curves: cell (column, row) draws the parametric curve x = A·sin(a·t + π/2), y = A·sin(b·t) with frequencies a = column + 1 and b = row + 1 (A = 0.4 cell, t from 0 to 2π, approximated by a 160-segment polyline), so the 16 ratios run from the 1:1 circle to 4:4; when a and b share a factor the curve retraces itself, and cases such as a = 2b degenerate into a parabola arc. Lines are bright green (half width 0.014 cell) with a soft glow on a deep-green panel, a faint cell frame and center cross graticule, and faint grain. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: oscilloscope screen curvature, phosphor trails, scan lines and readout text are not modeled, the phase is fixed at π/2 (it does not vary per cell), and the color is just a green tone interpolated linearly between the dark and light ends. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.002 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger; every sample of this material checks a 160-segment polyline, so it costs more than most materials: a one-off local Bun run sampled 512² in about 155 ms (versus about 56 ms for tumblingblocks and 17 ms for brick in the same run; not a benchmark, and neither browser nor Worker was measured); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`zoneplates` adds Fresnel zone plates, bringing the current catalog from 931 to 932 kinds (933 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds a 2×2 array of plates with 14 zones each; the outer edge of zone k lies at radius R·√(k/N) (R = 0.42 cell, N = 14), the Fresnel zone-plate radius law (every zone has equal area); even zones (including the central disc) are open (light) and odd zones opaque (near-black), the outermost 14th zone is opaque and meets a mid-gray backing, and a light-gray retaining ring surrounds each plate. Zone edges are slightly softened and the backing and ring carry faint grain. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only the geometry of the radius law is drawn — diffraction, focus, wavelength and intensity distribution are not simulated, and the ring count and size are normalized (R and N fixed), not tied to a real design wavelength or focal length. Defaults are a 0.2 m nominal tile, 0.0022 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so the outermost zone (about 0.015 cell wide) spans about 8 px (about 4 px at 512); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`backgammonboard` adds a backgammon board, bringing the current catalog from 932 to 933 kinds (934 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one board: inside a frame, two halves of 6 "points" (triangles) each around a darker central bar; the 12 triangles of the top row point down and the 12 of the bottom row point up, with alternating tones along each side (0.52 and 0.34) and the two rows offset by one point; triangles are 40% of the board height long. Each side has 15 checkers (light and dark), placed at random on the 24 points with at most 5 per point and only one color per point, stacked from the board edge toward the middle, with a rim highlight and a slight rise. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the checkers are a "legal random placement", not the standard opening or any real game position, and there are no dice, doubling cube, checkers on the bar or borne off; the total is fixed at 15 per side, so in every seed all checkers sit on points. When the tile repeats, each board is an independent game. Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.003 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so a checker spans about 34 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`jigsawpuzzle` adds a jigsaw puzzle, bringing the current catalog from 933 to 934 kinds (935 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds a 6×6-piece puzzle on a torus: every piece is based on a grid cell, and each edge between cells carries one round bulb (radius 0.115 cell, neck half width 0.05 cell, protruding 0.15 cell) whose owning side is chosen at random from the seed and which fits into the other side, so two neighboring pieces always interlock as one bulb and one socket; edges wrap at the tile boundary, so the repeated puzzle is continuous. Pieces are separated by thin cut lines with a small bevel; the picture is a low-frequency cloudy light-and-dark "image" (6×6 cells) with 48×48-cell detail, ±0.06 per-piece tone variation and grain. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the bulb is a circle on a straight neck, not the curves of a real die-cut; board thickness, printed picture content and the back of the pieces are not modeled. Piece areas differ a little because of bulbs and sockets (a piece whose bulbs and sockets cancel pairwise has plain cell area). Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.0026 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger so a piece spans about 170 px and a cut line about 5 px (about 2 px at 512); every sample runs roughly 40 ownership tests, so it is costlier than most materials: a one-off local Bun run sampled 512² in about 226 ms (versus about 122 ms for lissajousgrid, 45 ms for tumblingblocks and 16 ms for brick in the same run; not a benchmark, and neither browser nor Worker was measured); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`ramennoodles` adds ramen noodles, bringing the current catalog from 934 to 935 kinds (936 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 22 wavy noodle strands running along u, layered from top to bottom with later strands lying over earlier ones: the center line of strand i is y = (i + 0.5)/22 + 0.085·sin(2π(k·u + phase)), with k cycling through 3, 4, 5 and the phase taken from the golden ratio (the fractional part of i × 0.618…), so the strand shapes are the same for every seed and only tone and grain change with the seed; strands have a half width of 0.026 and a round tube profile (bright along the center line, darker toward the edges with lower height and ambient occlusion), ±0.06 per-strand tone variation, and about 7% of the area shows dark broth between strands. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: there are no curled noodles, breaks, oil slicks, toppings or broth reflections; because every strand is a wave with a fixed stacking order, the pile looks fairly regular. Distance to a strand uses a first-order approximation (vertical offset over √(1 + slope²)), so strand width is slightly off where the slope is steep at the crests. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.01 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger; each sample checks 22 strands, so the cost is moderate (a one-off local Bun run sampled 512² in about 200 ms, not a benchmark); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`connectfourboard` adds a Connect Four board, bringing the current catalog from 935 to 936 kinds (937 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one 7-column × 6-row board with one extra row of base tray below: a blue panel with round holes, each hole dark (empty) or holding the first player's disc (mid tone) or the second player's disc (near-white), with an inner ring and a slightly raised rim. The position comes from simulating a legal game from the seed: the two players alternate dropping discs into random columns (the first player starts), discs stack from the bottom by gravity, and a drop that would complete four in a row is rerolled into another column; there are 18–30 moves. So every column's discs are unbroken from the bottom, the first player has either as many discs as the second or exactly one more, and nobody has completed four in a row. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: there is no real red and yellow (colors are brightness interpolated linearly between the dark and light ends, so the first player is a mid blue-gray and the second near-white); the position is a random legal one, not a played strategy, and nothing guarantees that both sides would "sensibly" have played it. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so a hole spans about 73 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`bingocard` adds a bingo card, bringing the current catalog from 936 to 937 kinds (938 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is 6×6 cells; the left 5 columns × 6 rows hold one bingo card: the first row is a pale-gray "B I N G O" header (3×5 pixel font), and the five rows below hold numbers in the standard ranges — column B 1–15, I 16–30, N 31–45, G 46–60, O 61–75 — distinct within each column and chosen at random from the seed; the middle cell (third row of the N column) is the free space, marked by a gray dot; about 25% of the numbered cells carry a daub circle. The card has a heavier outer frame; the sixth column on the right is blank margin so repeated cards are separated. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the numbers do not represent any actual call state (the daubs are random, are not consistent with any call sequence and no win is checked); the font is a 3×5 pixel font with no printed artwork, card number or advertising. The header letters B, I, N, G and O are pixel approximations. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.0014 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so each cell spans about 85 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`pegsolitaire` adds peg solitaire, bringing the current catalog from 937 to 938 kinds (939 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one 7×7 English cross board: the four 2×2 corners are solid wood with no holes, the other 33 cells are holes holding either nothing (dark) or a light round-headed wooden peg (with a highlight and a small dome). The position comes from the seed by starting at the solved end state (one peg in the center) and playing 8 random moves backwards (each reverse jump turns "peg, empty, empty" in a line into "empty, peg, peg"), so the picture — 9 pegs, or fewer if the attempt budget runs out — can always be solved to a single peg by playing the jumps forward. The board has wood grain stretched along u. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the position is guaranteed only to be solvable (it comes from reverse play), not to be the shortest solution or a representative puzzle; only the 33-hole English board is drawn, not the 37-hole French board or other variants; colors are brightness interpolated linearly between the dark and light ends, so the pegs are a pale tan rather than a specific color. Defaults are a 0.35 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger so a cell spans about 73 px; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`xylophonebars` adds xylophone bars, bringing the current catalog from 938 to 939 kinds (940 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds 12 wooden bars side by side (the twelve semitones of an octave), all centered on the middle line of the tile; the first is the longest (0.9 tile) and each following bar is 2^(−1/24) ≈ 0.9715 of the previous one — a tuned bar's frequency goes as 1/length², so one equal-tempered semitone (frequency ratio 2^(1/12)) shortens it by 2^(−1/24), and twelve semitones (an octave) by 2^(−1/2). Each bar has a suspension hole 22.4% in from each end (the node positions of the first free–free bending mode), and the holes of neighboring bars are joined by a thin line (the cord); the wood grain runs along the bar and the bar tone lightens slightly from long to short on a dark felt background. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only the length ratios and node positions are drawn — bar thickness, width and cross-section (real xylophone bars are often undercut to tune the overtones) are not modeled, nor are resonator tubes; the cord is a straight line between holes and snaps back at the tile seam (from the last bar to the next tile's first bar), a deliberate discontinuity. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.0035 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`sliderulescale` adds slide-rule scales, bringing the current catalog from 939 to 940 kinds (941 gallery choices including plain). Each tile stacks 8 ivory logarithmic C scales, one decade spanning the whole tile width: the graduation x is drawn at u = log10(x), so 10 coincides with the 1 of the next tile and the tile repeats seamlessly. Graduations follow the usual rule — every 0.02 in [1, 2), every 0.05 in [2, 4), every 0.1 in [4, 10) — for 150 ticks at three lengths: integers (9, longest), tenths in [1, 4) and halves in [4, 10) (33 medium) and the remaining 108 shortest; each integer tick has a 3×5 dot-matrix digit 1 to 9 to its right. Each scale has a thin line along its top and bottom, the ink varies slightly in density and the paper has a fine grain. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only a single decade of the C scale is drawn — no other scales (A/B/D/CI/K/S/T/L), no cursor, no markings or lettering; digits use a 3×5 dot matrix rather than a printed typeface; the graduation rule is the common one and not a reproduction of a particular maker's model; colors are linear brightness interpolation (ivory base, near-black ink) and do not represent real material hues. Defaults are a 0.25 m nominal tile, 0.002 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger (the closest ticks are only about 0.0044 apart in u); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`spirographrosettes` adds spirograph rosettes, bringing the current catalog from 940 to 941 kinds (942 gallery choices including plain). Each tile draws one hypotrochoid per quadrant (the curve traced by a pen at some distance from the center of a small gear rolling inside a large ring) with ring/gear radius ratios 5/3, 8/3, 7/2 and 9/4, giving 5, 8, 7 and 9 lobes (for coprime R and r the lobe count equals R); the curve closes after t has run through 2π·r/gcd(R, r). The pen distance varies with the seed and quadrant between 0.37 and 0.73 gear radii and each figure is rotated by the seed; the pen always reaches exactly the outer circle (radius 0.215 tile), so all lobe tips lie on one circle. The four quadrants use slightly different ink brightness and the paper has a fine grain. Metallic and mortar are 0.

Implementation: when the sampler is created the curves are stamped (about 108 thousand points) into a 640×640 Float32 ink-coverage mask (about 1.6 MB, one per sampler) and every sample is just a bilinear lookup; the mask resolution is fixed at 640, so above 640 px the lines are magnified by the bilinear filter and get slightly softer rather than sharper.

This is an authored schematic: only four fixed ring/gear ratios are drawn (not arbitrary R, r), the curve is a polyline of uniformly spaced t samples (9000 points per loop, not an exact distance field); colors are brightness interpolation (near-black ink on cream paper) and do not represent pen hues. Defaults are a 0.3 m nominal tile, 0.0012 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 to 1024; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`juliacells` adds Julia-set cells, bringing the current catalog from 941 to 942 kinds (943 gallery choices including plain). Each tile draws one filled Julia set z → z² + c per quadrant with a real c (about −0.4, −0.75, −1.0 and −1.25, each jittered by up to ±0.04 by the seed; for real c in [−2, 1/4] the set is connected and the picture is mirror symmetric in both axes). Each cell's view radius is 2, equal to the escape radius, so points on a cell border escape immediately and there is no seam between cells or at the tile repeat. The interior (points still bounded after 96 iterations) is near black; outside, the smooth escape count μ = n + 1 − log₂(ln|z| / ln 2) drives a glow 1 − exp(−μ/9) from dark to bright, and height and roughness follow the same glow. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only four real-parameter Julia sets — no Mandelbrot set and no complex c (a complex c is not mirror symmetric and would leave a seam at the tile border); the iteration cap is 96, so slowly escaping points near the boundary are drawn as interior; colors are brightness interpolation (near-black navy to warm cream) and do not represent any particular palette. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.0035 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`nonogrampuzzle` adds a nonogram (picture-logic puzzle), bringing the current catalog from 942 to 943 kinds (944 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a 12×12-cell sheet: the bottom-right 8×8 is the solved picture (about 55% of the cells are filled with dark squares, chosen by the seed), and the 4 columns to its left and 4 rows above it carry the run-length clues of every row and column, printed as 3×5 dot-matrix digits (0 to 8) in grey clue cells — row clues right-aligned against the picture, column clues bottom-aligned above it; an empty line prints "0"; the top-left 4×4 is blank paper. Grid lines are heavier every five cells and around the border. The clues and the picture come from the same fill data, so they always agree. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the picture is a random fill and is not guaranteed to have a unique solution (a real nonogram needs one — this draws a solved sheet only); the size is fixed at 8×8 with at most 4 clues per line (8 cells allow at most 4 runs); digits are a single digit; colors are brightness interpolation (near-black ink on cream paper) and do not represent any printing ink. Defaults are a 0.36 m nominal tile, 0.0016 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger (about 43 px per cell); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`lightsoutboard` adds a Lights Out board, bringing the current catalog from 943 to 944 kinds (945 gallery choices including plain). Each tile holds one 5×5 board of rounded-square buttons: lit ones are warm yellow with a highlight and a glow toward the middle, unlit ones dark brown, separated by a dark plastic frame; lit buttons sit slightly lower and are glossier. The lit pattern is what you get from the all-off board by pressing about 40% of the buttons chosen by the seed (each press toggles the button and its four orthogonal neighbors), so the position is always solvable — only one in four arbitrary 5×5 Lights Out boards is. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only the standard 5×5 rules are drawn (no other sizes or variants), as a static position with no move order or solution hint; the lit color is brightness interpolation (near-black to warm yellow) and the buttons do not correspond to a real hardware material. Defaults are a 0.25 m nominal tile, 0.003 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 256 or larger (about 51 px per button); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`weavingdraft` adds a weaving draft, bringing the current catalog from 944 to 945 kinds (946 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a 16×16-cell sheet showing the full draft for a four-shaft loom: the 12×4 block at the top left is the threading (which shaft each warp thread runs through, one mark per column, shaft 1 in the row nearest the drawdown), the 4×4 block at the top right is the tie-up (which shafts each treadle lifts), the 12×4 block at the bottom right is the treadling (which treadle is pressed for each weft pass, one mark per row), and the 12×12 block at the bottom left is the drawdown they determine: a dark cell means that warp thread floats on top for that pass (its shaft is lifted), a light cell means the weft is on top. Threading and treadling are chosen at random by the seed, and every treadle lifts a non-empty, non-full set of shafts (1 to 3), so the drawdown is fully fixed by the rule. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only four shafts, four treadles and the 12×12 rising-shed convention are drawn; threading and treadling are random rather than a traditional twill or satin rule, so the drawdown can look untidy; there is no yarn color (dark/light only means warp float/weft float) and no cloth-structure relief; colors are brightness interpolation (near-black ink on cream paper) and do not represent any yarn hue. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.0018 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger (32 px per cell); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`scrambledcubenet` adds the unfolded net of a scrambled Rubik-style cube, bringing the current catalog from 945 to 946 kinds (947 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a 14×14-cell sheet with a cross-shaped net of a 3×3×3 twisty cube in the middle (the up, left, front, right, back and down faces, 54 stickers over 12×9 cells); the stickers are rounded squares set in a black plastic body, and the six face colors are six brightnesses (0.24 to 0.95). The cube is scrambled by the seed with 22 random quarter turns of the outer layers (rotating the 3D positions and normals, not random coloring), so the picture is always a legal, solvable cube state: each brightness appears on exactly 9 stickers, the six centers are six different brightnesses, each of the 12 edge pieces and 8 corner pieces appears exactly once, and no piece pairs colors from opposite faces. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the net uses the common cross layout (U above F, D below F, L/R/B beside and behind F), not any particular notation; the colors are brightness, not real cube hues, and the six brightnesses are only about 0.12 to 0.16 apart, so they are hard to tell apart at small sizes; no 3D cube shape is drawn; 22 random quarter turns do not guarantee a uniformly random state. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.0028 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 512 or larger (about 37 px per cell); 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`knightstour` adds a knight's tour, bringing the current catalog from 946 to 947 kinds (948 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is an 8×8 chessboard of alternating light and dark squares, every square numbered 1 to 64 in 3×5 dot-matrix digits along an open knight's tour (each step an L-shaped knight move, no square visited twice), with a faint thin line joining the centers of consecutively numbered squares. The tour is found when the sampler is created by depth-first search that tries the square with the fewest onward moves first (Warnsdorff's rule), breaking ties by the seed; each attempt has a node budget (40000) and a failure restarts from another square (up to 60 attempts); the start and the route vary with the seed. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only an open route is drawn, not required to return to the start (not a closed tour); there are no pieces; where the thin line crosses digits the digits are drawn on top of it; the search is a budgeted heuristic, not exhaustive — on 600 seeds a complete 64-step route was in fact found every time (the slowest creation was about 49 ms, a one-off local measurement and not a benchmark), but that does not prove every seed succeeds; if all 60 attempts failed, the squares reached would be numbered and the rest left blank (not observed here).
