`moonphases` adds a moon-phase calendar, bringing the current catalog from 1012 to 1013 kinds (1014 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a calendar of 30 moons, 5 columns by 6 rows on a deep-blue night sky, standing for 30 consecutive days of the synodic month (29.53 days): new moon, first quarter, full moon, last quarter and back to a thin crescent. Each moon is a grey disc with the same 24 random craters (it is the same Moon), the lit part pale and the shadow dark grey; waxing moons (the first half of the cycle) are lit from the right and waning moons from the left, with an elliptical terminator, and the lit share of the disc is (1−cos 2πφ)/2 where φ is the phase within the cycle. The calendar starts at a seed-driven fraction of a day, so different seeds differ slightly in phase. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic, not an ephemeris: phase uses only the simple cosine formula (a fixed synodic month of 29.53 days), with no libration, earthshine or real lunar terrain; the view is northern-hemisphere (waxing lit on the right), and a southern-hemisphere observer sees the mirror image; 30 days cover a bit more than one 29.53-day cycle, so the first and last moons have close but not identical phases; colour is brightness only. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`treemaprects` adds a treemap, bringing the current catalog from 1013 to 1014 kinds (1015 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a treemap: a square area cut into 12 rectangles whose **areas are proportional to their values**, each value (an integer from 4 to 30, chosen by the seed) written in 3×5 dot-matrix digits in the rectangle's top-left corner; the larger the value, the lighter the fill (a linear map onto the dark-to-light ramp). The layout: the values are sorted in descending order and split into two groups of nearly equal total (each next value goes to the group with the smaller total so far), the current rectangle is cut across its longer side in the proportion of the two totals, and each half is handled recursively until one value is left; dark thick lines separate the rectangles. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: it is not the usual "squarified" treemap algorithm, only balanced binary recursion, so the rectangles' aspect ratios are not guaranteed to be near square (I did not measure the worst aspect ratio); the values are random and carry no real data; the labels are a fixed-size dot matrix and a very narrow rectangle could let a label spill over its edge (the tests only confirm that the labels of nine seeds read back correctly and do not check that a label fits entirely inside its rectangle, nor is it proven that it cannot overflow); colour is brightness only. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`analogclocks` adds analogue clocks, bringing the current catalog from 1014 to 1015 kinds (1016 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a 4×4 wall of clocks: 16 round faces on a grey wall (pale face, dark rim, twelve tick marks), each showing a seed-chosen time (1 to 12 o'clock, whole minutes 0 to 59). The hands are set the way a real clock turns them: the minute hand 6° per minute; the hour hand 30° per hour plus 0.5° per minute, so the hour hand creeps with the minutes instead of sitting at the hour position. The hour hand is shorter and thicker (0.5 of the face radius), the minute hand longer and thinner (0.8), with a small hub at the centre. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: there is no second hand, no numerals and no AM/PM distinction; there are only 12 ticks and no minute ticks; the hands are plain straight bars with no ornamental shapes; each face is random independently so the times on one wall are not related; colour is brightness only. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.005 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`piechartwedges` adds a pie chart, bringing the current catalog from 1015 to 1016 kinds (1017 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a pie chart: a circle cut into 8 wedges whose **central angle equals the wedge's percentage × 3.6°**, each percentage (at least 4%, all eight adding up to exactly 100%) written in 3×5 dot-matrix digits in the middle of the wedge. The seed decides the percentages: each wedge first gets a base of 4%, the remaining 68% is shared out by random weights, and the largest-remainder rule makes the total exactly 100. The first wedge starts at a seed-driven angle and the others follow clockwise; every wedge has its own brightness (by size rank, a linear map from dark to light, ties broken by position); wedges and the rim are separated by thin dark lines, and the digits are dark or light depending on the fill. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the percentages are random and carry no data meaning; the smallest wedge is 4% (about 14.4°) and the labels are a fixed-size dot matrix, so a small wedge's label may sit close to its edges; brightness is only the size rank, with no hue; there is no legend, title or axis. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`regressionscatter` adds a regression scatter plot, bringing the current catalog from 1016 to 1017 kinds (1018 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a scatter plot: on pale paper a square plot area (thin frame, a light 4×4 grid) holds up to 28 dark dots scattered round a line whose slope and intercept come from the seed (each point's noise is the sum of three uniform randoms, an approximation of a normal distribution), no two dots closer than 0.075 (in plot units); the **least-squares** regression line is then fitted to those dots (slope = Σ(x−x̄)(y−ȳ) / Σ(x−x̄)², intercept = ȳ − slope·x̄) and drawn across the whole plot as a thinner mid-grey line. Because the fit is computed from the dots actually drawn, the line always passes through their centroid. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: because the minimum-spacing rejection sampling has an attempt cap the point count can fall below 28 (I did not tally the actual counts per seed; the tests only require at least 15); there are no tick numerals, title or legend; no correlation coefficient or residuals are computed; the noise is approximately but not strictly normal; colour is brightness only (dark dots, a mid-grey line, light grid lines). Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`barchartbars` adds a bar chart, bringing the current catalog from 1017 to 1018 kinds (1019 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a bar chart: on pale paper a dark baseline with 10 bars side by side, each a **whole number of units** high (a unit is 0.075 of the tile height, values 1 to 9 chosen by the seed), with the value written above the top of each bar in centred 3×5 dot-matrix digits; there is a very faint horizontal rule at every whole height; each bar's fill follows its value linearly along the dark-to-light ramp (larger values lighter). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: there are no axis tick numerals, title or legend; all bars share one height unit; the values are random and carry no data meaning; bars reach at most 9 units (about 0.675 of the tile height); colour is brightness only. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`truthtablelogic` adds a truth table, bringing the current catalog from 1018 to 1019 kinds (1020 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is the truth table of a four-input Boolean function: a dark header row reads A, B, C, D (inputs) and X (output) in 3×5 dot-matrix letters (the X header is slightly lighter), and below it 16 rows count ABCD in binary (0000 to 1111, A the most significant bit) with the output X in the last column; cells where X is 1 have a darker background so they stand out. The seed picks the output from eight functions: (A∧B)∨(C⊕D), (A⊕B)∧(C∨D), A?B:C (a multiplexer, independent of D), ¬(A∨B)∨(C∧D), (A∨C)∧(B∨D), majority(A,B,C)⊕D, (A∧¬B)∨(C∧¬D), and 4-bit parity (A⊕B⊕C⊕D). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only those eight fixed functions occur (not arbitrary truth tables); darkening the cells where the output is 1 is decoration; no simplified Boolean expression or logic-gate symbol is drawn; the dot-matrix digits and letters are hard to read at small sizes. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`fouriersquarewaves` adds Fourier square waves, bringing the current catalog from 1019 to 1020 kinds (1021 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is six horizontal panels stacked: panel n draws the partial sum of the first n odd harmonics of the square wave's Fourier series, (4/π)·Σ sin(kt)/k (k = 1, 3, …, 2n−1). Panel 1 is a plain sine (amplitude 4/π); each added harmonic flattens the plateaus further but puts more ripple on them and steepens the transitions, while the overshoot of about 9% of the jump on either side of an edge (the Gibbs phenomenon) does not go away and tends to about 1.179 times the square wave's level. Each panel has a faint axis and two reference rules at the ideal square wave's levels, the waveform is a dark curve (its width is corrected for slope so steep stretches do not thin out), and each tile spans 2 periods horizontally. The content is fixed; the seed only affects faint paper noise. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only 1 to 6 harmonics are drawn, none higher; the curve is computed from the analytic formula per pixel, not plotted from points; the vertical scale is fixed with no axis labels; the "about 9% overshoot" is the theoretical Gibbs value, and the tests only verify that the measured maximum lies between 1.1 and 1.2 rather than equalling it exactly. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`pascaltrianglenumbers` adds Pascal triangle numbers, bringing the current catalog from 1020 to 1021 kinds (1022 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is the first 10 rows of Pascal's triangle (rows 0 to 9, the largest entry being 126) in 3×5 dot-matrix digits laid out as a centred triangle; each entry is the sum of the upper-left and upper-right entries of the row above, and odd entries sit on a darker disc while even entries are bare paper, so the odd discs trace out the Sierpinski triangle pattern. The content is fixed and the seed only affects faint paper noise. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only the first 10 rows are drawn; the odd/even discs and the digits are deterministic; no binomial expansion or row sums are labelled; the dot-matrix digits are hard to read at small sizes. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`convexhullpoints` adds a convex hull, bringing the current catalog from 1021 to 1022 kinds (1023 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a scatter of points (up to 16, at least 0.12 UV apart and at least 0.07 UV from the tile edge so the tile repeats seamlessly) with their convex hull: hull vertices are marked with larger dark dots, inner points with smaller dots, and the hull polygon is filled light grey with a thin outline. The hull of the seed's point set is computed with Andrew's monotone chain. To keep the hull unambiguous the sampler picks a point set in which no vertex is nearly collinear with its two neighbours and no inner point hugs an edge (up to 48 candidate sets are tried; if none qualifies the last is used). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: at most 16 points (the minimum spacing means some seeds place fewer); only the hull is shown, not the algorithm's steps; "unambiguous" is a threshold I chose (a vertex at least 0.012 UV from the line through its neighbours, an inner point at least 0.02 UV from any edge), not a mathematical requirement; if all 48 candidates fail the last is used anyway (this caused no test failure across the 600 seeds I tried, but I did not count how often all 48 are used up). Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`delaunaytriangles` adds a Delaunay triangulation, bringing the current catalog from 1022 to 1023 kinds (1024 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a scatter of points (up to 18, at least 0.1 UV apart and at least 0.06 UV from the tile edge so the tile repeats seamlessly) joined into their Delaunay triangulation: each triangle is filled with a slightly varied light grey, the edges are thin lines and the vertices small dark dots. The triangulation is found by brute force: a triple of points is a Delaunay triangle when no other point lies strictly inside its circumcircle (a few hundred triples times the remaining points, computed once when the sampler is created). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only the resulting triangulation is drawn, without circumcircles or the algorithm's steps; at most 18 points (the minimum spacing means some seeds place fewer); the positions are seed-driven pseudo-random and near-ties (four nearly cocircular points) are not specially handled, so whether such a triple counts as Delaunay depends on floating-point error and overlapping triangles can in principle appear, which I did not guard against; each pixel scans about 30 triangles and 47 edges, so it costs more per pixel than most materials (about 0.4 s for a single 512×512 render under Bun). Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`collatzsequences` adds Collatz sequences, bringing the current catalog from 1023 to 1024 kinds (1025 gallery choices including plain). Each tile has 10 rows, each the Collatz (hailstone) sequence of a start value: halve an even number, otherwise triple it and add one, until 1 is reached. Every number takes one cell (up to 13 cells), written in 3×5 dot-matrix digits; cells holding odd numbers have a darker background so the "triple and add one" rises are visible, and the rest of the row after the sequence ends is left blank. The seed shuffles the 21 start values from 2 to 60 whose sequences have at most 13 terms and takes 10 of them (all different), for example 3, 13, 21, 53 or 40. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only start values with sequences of at most 13 terms appear (no long trajectories such as 27's), so it says nothing about the Collatz conjecture itself; numbers have at most three digits (53's sequence contains 160); the dot-matrix digits are hard to read at small sizes. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`hammingcodewords` adds Hamming code words, bringing the current catalog from 1024 to 1025 kinds (1026 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a Hamming(7,4) code word table: the header reads positions 1 to 7 and S (the syndrome), and below it 16 rows hold the 7-bit code word for the data nibbles 0000 to 1111 (row number r) with positions 1 to 7 being p1 p2 d1 p3 d2 d3 d4 (the parity bits at positions 1, 2 and 4 on a slightly darker background). The seed decides for each row whether one bit is flipped (about one row in eight is left alone), and the flipped cell has the darkest background; the rightmost column S is the syndrome (the XOR of the positions holding a 1 in the received word), which equals the flipped position exactly, with 0 meaning no error. So flipping back the bit that S points to gives the original code word, whose data bits are the row number. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only the Hamming(7,4) code, with the 16 rows fixed to data 0 to 15, and at most one flipped bit per row (a Hamming code cannot correct two-bit errors, so none are drawn); the flipped positions are seed-driven pseudo-random; the dot-matrix digits are hard to read at small sizes. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`cayleytables` adds Cayley tables, bringing the current catalog from 1025 to 1026 kinds (1027 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is the Cayley (multiplication) table of a group of order 8: a 9×9 grid whose top row and left column are dark headers listing the elements 0 to 7, and in the remaining 8×8 body the cell in row a, column b is the product of a and b; cells holding 0 (the identity) have a darker background, tracing the Latin-square pattern of the identity. The seed picks one of the five groups of order 8: the cyclic group Z8, Z4×Z2, Z2×Z2×Z2, the dihedral group D4 and the quaternion group Q8, and shuffles the labels of elements 1 to 7 (0 stays the identity). Every table therefore satisfies the group axioms: an identity, every row and column a permutation, and associativity; the table's shape shows whether the group is commutative and the order of each element. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only those five groups of order 8, with no other orders and no group names shown; the shuffled labels hide any regular pattern; the dot-matrix digits are hard to read at small sizes. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`romannumerals` adds Roman numerals, bringing the current catalog from 1026 to 1027 kinds (1028 gallery choices including plain). Each tile has 14 rows: on the left a decimal number (1 to 199, right-aligned in three cells), then an equals sign and its Roman numeral (in 3×5 dot-matrix letters I, V, X, L and C, in the standard subtractive form, e.g. 4 as IV, 9 as IX, 40 as XL, 90 as XC; flush left, at most nine letters, e.g. 188 as CLXXXVIII); odd rows have a slightly darker background. The seed picks the 14 numbers from 1 to 199, all different and in ascending order. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only up to 199 (no D or M, so nothing from 500 up); only the standard subtractive notation, not the ancient variants such as IIII; the dot-matrix letters are hard to read at small sizes (I and the digit 1 have different shapes but can still be confused when tiny). Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`signedencodings` adds signed integer encodings, bringing the current catalog from 1027 to 1028 kinds (1029 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a 4-bit lookup table: 16 rows list 0000 to 1111, and each row has the binary bits (the column of the most significant bit, the sign bit, has the darkest background), the unsigned value (0 to 15, header U) and the signed value (header: the encoding's code). The seed picks one of three signed encodings: two's complement (2C, where 1000 is −8 up to 1111 as −1), one's complement (1C, where 1000 is −7 and 1111 is "−0") and sign and magnitude (SM, where 1000 is "−0" and 1001 is −1 up to 1111 as −7); negative rows (including "−0") have a slightly darker background and a "−" sign written before the signed value. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only 4 bits and only those three encodings, with no overflow or arithmetic shown; one's complement and sign-magnitude have a negative zero while two's complement does not, which is the point of putting them side by side; the dot-matrix digits are hard to read at small sizes. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`matrixproducts` adds matrix products, bringing the current catalog from 1028 to 1029 kinds (1030 gallery choices including plain). Each tile stacks 4 equations, each a 3×3 matrix multiplication A × B = C: the 9 entries of A and of B are single digits 0 to 9 drawn by the seed, and C is their matrix product (entries of up to three digits, such as 141 or 153). Each matrix has square brackets around it, the cells of the product C have a slightly darker background, and the times and equals signs sit on the middle row. The digits are written in 3×5 dot matrices. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only 3×3 with entries 0 to 9; no working is shown, and nothing excludes zero rows or an all-zero matrix; the dot-matrix digits are hard to read at small sizes (the gaps between three-digit product cells are narrow). Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`kochsnowflake` adds a Koch snowflake, bringing the current catalog from 1029 to 1030 kinds (1031 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is divided into four 2×2 panels showing the Koch snowflake after 0, 1, 2 and 3 iterations: 0 is an equilateral triangle, and each iteration replaces the middle third of every edge with an outward equilateral bump. The panels' order and each one's rotation are drawn by the seed, and each polygon (up to 192 segments) is filled by the even-odd rule and outlined with a thin dark line. The boundary length therefore grows by 4/3 per iteration and the area follows A₀·(8/5 − (3/5)(4/9)ⁿ) (A₀ at 0, tending to 1.6·A₀). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only 0 to 3 iterations are drawn, none higher, and it is not the true fractal limit; the side length of 0.36 UV (circumradius about 0.208 UV) is fixed and the panels do not overlap; each pixel scans up to 192 edges, about 0.3 s for one 512×512 render under Bun (from the preview script, measured once). Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`newtonbasins` adds Newton basins, bringing the current catalog from 1030 to 1031 kinds (1032 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is the basin-of-attraction picture of Newton's method for z^n − 1 (the n-th roots of unity): a square window of the complex plane from −1.6 to 1.6 in both real and imaginary parts, where each point is painted with the brightness of the n-th root that the Newton iteration z ← z − (z^n − 1)/(n z^(n−1)) finally converges to (more roots mean more brightness levels), with slowly converging points slightly darker (by up to 10 steps). The seed picks n from 3, 4 and 5; the coordinates pass through a triangle-wave fold, so the pattern is continuous across the tile edge. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only the polynomials z^n − 1 with n from 3 to 5; brightness only tells which root and how fast, not hue (procedural materials only have a dark-to-light ramp); basin boundaries are fractal, so pixels near them are sensitive to floating-point error, each pixel iterates at most 40 times and a point that does not converge (near a boundary or a pole) is assigned by its final position; the triangle-wave fold mirrors the pattern left-right and top-bottom. Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`mandelbrotbands` adds Mandelbrot bands, bringing the current catalog from 1031 to 1032 kinds (1033 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a smooth escape-time contour picture of the Mandelbrot set: the seed picks one of four fixed windows — the whole set (centre −0.6, half-width 1.6, 80 iterations), the seahorse valley (−0.745 + 0.113i, half-width 0.06), a branch near the imaginary axis (−0.16 + 1.0405i, half-width 0.04) and the neighbourhood of the Feigenbaum point (−1.401155, half-width 0.02), the last three zoomed windows with 160 iterations each. The inside of the set (points that do not escape within the iteration limit) is dark; outside, each point's brightness comes from the smooth escape time ν = n + 1 − log₂(log|z| / log 256) (larger ν is brighter, with a cosine contour band of period 3 added), so rings of bands surround the set. The coordinates pass through a triangle-wave fold so the pattern is continuous across the tile edge. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only those four windows, with no interactive zoom; the inside is decided by the iteration limit, so pixels on the set's edge can differ slightly with the limit (in a temporary test over 120 extra seeds the best window's agreement was 1.0000 (the runner-up at most 0.265), at a 0.02 tolerance on an 80×80 grid, which does not mean every pixel agrees); brightness only encodes escape speed, not hue; the triangle-wave fold mirrors the pattern. Each pixel iterates at most 160 times, about 0.15 s for one 512×512 render under Bun (from the preview script, measured once). Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`cantorsetrows` adds Cantor set rows, bringing the current catalog from 1032 to 1033 kinds (1034 gallery choices including plain). Each tile stacks 8 horizontal bands from top to bottom, the k-th (k = 0 to 7) being the set after k steps of the Cantor construction: band 0 is the whole strip and each further step removes the middle part of every interval. The seed picks one of three fractions removed: a third (the classic Cantor set, where band k has 2^k intervals of length (1/3)^k), a fifth (two fifths kept each step, leaving more) and a half (a quarter kept each step, leaving less); band k has 2^k equal intervals with total length (1 − r)^k, r being the fraction removed. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only 8 steps (at step 7 the intervals are narrower than a pixel and only faint stripes show) and only those three removal fractions; each band's edge is softened by distance to the interval boundary; the paper shows between bands. Defaults are a 0.8 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`conicsections` adds conic sections, bringing the current catalog from 1033 to 1034 kinds (1035 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is divided into four 2×2 panels showing a circle, an ellipse, a parabola and a hyperbola with eccentricity e of 0, 0.6, 1 and 1.6, the arrangement of the panels being chosen by the seed. The curves are drawn from the definition "distance to the focus r = e × distance to the directrix" (the semi-latus rectum l is fixed at 0.09 UV; written r = |l − e·x'|, where x' is the offset from the focus along the axis); the focus is a small dot and the directrix (for e > 0) a dashed line; the parabola and hyperbola run to the panel edge and both branches of the hyperbola are drawn. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only those four curves, with the axis fixed horizontal (no rotation) and no axes or labels; the line width is softened by a first-order distance estimate of the implicit function, so it can vary slightly on steep stretches far from the vertex; each panel's focus position is set by hand to centre the curve (the hyperbola's focus sits left of centre and the ellipse's right). Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`rosecurves` adds rose curves, bringing the current catalog from 1034 to 1035 kinds (1036 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is nine 3×3 panels, each drawing a rhodonea rose r = R·cos(kθ) with k equal to 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 3/2, 5/2, 5/3 and 7/3 (an integer k gives k or 2k petals, a fractional k gives overlapping multi-petal roses, and k = 1 is a circle); the seed decides how the nine curves are arranged in the panels. Lines are drawn using a first-order distance estimate to the polar implicit function, and the origin is marked. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only those nine values of k, a fixed amplitude and no rotation phase; no formula or labels are shown; a fractional-k curve needs several turns to close (the denominator times 2π), and the sampler evaluates the distance estimate over twice the denominator half-turns for each pixel, so the line width can vary slightly where petals cross. Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`cassiniovals` adds Cassini ovals, bringing the current catalog from 1035 to 1036 kinds (1037 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is centred on two foci and draws a family of Cassini ovals: on each curve the product of the distances to the two foci, |PF₁|·|PF₂| = b², is constant, with b = ratio × a (a is half the distance between the foci, picked by the seed from 0.13, 0.18 and 0.23 UV) and the ratios 0.4, 0.7, 0.9, 1, 1.15, 1.35 and 1.6. For a ratio below 1 the curve splits into two ovals each enclosing one focus; for a ratio of 1 it is the lemniscate of Bernoulli (a figure eight, drawn thicker); above 1 it is a single curve enclosing both foci. The two foci are marked with small dots. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only those 7 ratios, always horizontal, with no axes or labels; the line width is softened by a first-order distance estimate of the implicit function (at the midpoint between the foci the gradient is zero, so I floor it at 1e-6 to avoid dividing by zero, see the log below); only three focal separations to choose from. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`twosourceripples` adds two-source ripples, bringing the current catalog from 1036 to 1037 kinds (1038 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is the interference pattern of two identical circular wave sources: the sources sit horizontally in the middle of the tile 0.22 or 0.32 UV apart, with a wavelength of 0.04, 0.052 or 0.07 UV (both picked by the seed), and each point's brightness comes from the sum of the two waves, cos(k·r₁) + cos(k·r₂) = 2 cos(k(r₁+r₂)/2) cos(k(r₁−r₂)/2) (r₁ and r₂ being the distances to the two sources), so on the hyperbolas where r₁ − r₂ is an odd multiple of half a wavelength the waves cancel and the brightness sits at its middle value (the nodal lines), while the crests and troughs are brightest or darkest where the waves are in step. The coordinates pass through a triangle-wave fold so the pattern is continuous across the tile edge. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only two point sources of equal amplitude and phase, with no attenuation or reflecting boundary (the triangle-wave fold is only for a continuous seam, not physical mirroring); brightness is just interference intensity, not hue. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.006 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview (a wavelength of 0.04 UV is only about 20 pixels at 512×512, and smaller sizes alias). This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`luhnnumbers` adds Luhn numbers, bringing the current catalog from 1037 to 1038 kinds (1039 gallery choices including plain). Each tile has 10 rows of 16-digit numbers (in groups of four with a gap between groups); the last digit of each row is the check digit (on a darker background) and each row ends with a mark: a check mark means the whole number passes the Luhn test (from the right, double every second digit, subtract 9 from any result above 9, and the total is a multiple of 10) and a cross means it does not. The first 15 digits of each row are drawn by the seed and the check digit is computed from them; about 60% of the rows are left valid and in the others one digit is changed to a different one (changing any single digit always breaks the Luhn sum), so the marks agree with the actual check result. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: the digits are random, not real card numbers (no issuer prefix or length rules are modelled), only an illustration of the Luhn algorithm; the share of valid rows in a tile comes from the seed's pseudo-randomness (about 60%, not a fixed count); odd rows (second, fourth, …) have a slightly darker background; the dot-matrix digits are hard to read at small sizes. Defaults are a 0.4 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`bezierconstruction` adds a Bezier construction, bringing the current catalog from 1038 to 1039 kinds (1040 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is divided into four 2×2 panels, each drawing a cubic Bezier curve (four fixed control points P₀ to P₃, in four different shapes, arranged by the seed) together with de Casteljau's construction at one parameter t: a dashed line joins the control polygon, the end points P₀ and P₃ are filled squares and the two inner control points P₁ and P₂ are hollow rings, then the first- and second-level segments obtained by interpolating neighbouring points at t are drawn as thin lines, and the final interpolated point, which lies on the curve, is marked with a filled dot. The seed picks t from 1/4, 1/3, 1/2 and 2/3, shared by all four panels. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only four fixed cubic curves and four fixed values of t; the curve itself is a 64-segment polyline approximation (each pixel takes the distance to 64 segments); the dashes only suggest the control polygon and their gaps come from a screen-space grid, not equal dashes along the line; about 0.2 s for one 512×512 render under Bun (from the preview script, measured once). Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`vennregions` adds Venn regions, bringing the current catalog from 1039 to 1040 kinds (1041 gallery choices including plain). Each tile draws a Venn diagram of three sets A, B and C: three equal circles arranged as an equilateral triangle, partly overlapping, form seven regions (only A, only B, only C, A and B, A and C, B and C, and all three), each with its element count in the middle (0 to 40, drawn by the seed, written in 3×5 dot-matrix digits); the background gets darker where circles overlap (the more circles a point lies in, the darker), and the circles are outlined with a thin dark line; the top left lists three totals "A=…", "B=…" and "C=…", each the sum of all regions inside that circle, so the size of the union can be checked by inclusion–exclusion. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only three sets and seven regions, with the circles' positions and sizes fixed; the counts are random, not real data; totals have at most three digits (for example A=105); the dot-matrix digits are hard to read at small sizes. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`ieee754floats` adds IEEE 754 single-precision floats, bringing the current catalog from 1040 to 1041 kinds (1042 gallery choices including plain). Each tile has six single-precision numbers from top to bottom: above each, a line with its decimal value (to 4 decimal places, in 3×5 dot-matrix digits with a decimal point and a minus sign), and below it its 32 bits: 1 sign bit (on the darkest background), 8 exponent bits (bias 127, on the next darkest) and 23 mantissa bits (on the lightest), with thin lines between the fields. The value is (−1)^sign × 2^(exponent−127) × (1 + mantissa/2²³); the seed draws each number's sign, exponent (124 to 133, so magnitudes roughly between 0.125 and 128) and mantissa, so the printed decimal and the bits correspond. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only normal single-precision numbers, with the exponent fixed to 124 to 133 and no zero, infinity, NaN or subnormals; the decimal value is printed to only 4 decimal places, the exact value rounded; the bits are drawn as 3×5 dot-matrix 0s and 1s and are hard to read at small sizes. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`bellcurvebands` adds bell-curve bands, bringing the current catalog from 1041 to 1042 kinds (1043 gallery choices including plain). Each tile draws the bell curve of a normal distribution (the Gaussian e^(−z²/2) with z = (x − mean)/σ), with the area under the curve split into four bands by how many standard deviations from the mean: within 1σ the darkest, 1 to 2σ the next, 2 to 3σ lighter and beyond 3σ the lightest; an axis runs along the bottom with a tick at each σ from −3σ to +3σ. The width σ is picked by the seed from 0.07, 0.09 and 0.11 UV, and the height of the curve is fixed. Because the area is proportional to the integral of e^(−z²/2), the ratio of the band areas is the normal distribution's 68–95–99.7 rule (about 68.27%, 95.45% and 99.73%). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: just one standard normal curve with σ chosen from only three values and no axis labels or numbers; the line width is corrected for slope; the bands use brightness only (procedural materials only have a dark-to-light ramp); the height is fixed, so curves with different σ enclose different areas (they are only similar in shape), which makes this an illustration of proportions rather than of absolute probability density. Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`roundingmodes` adds rounding modes, bringing the current catalog from 1042 to 1043 kinds (1044 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a rounding comparison table: a dark header row reads X and the codes of five ways of rounding — FL (floor), CE (ceiling), TR (truncation toward zero), RN (round to nearest, halves away from zero) and RE (round to nearest, halves to even, "banker's rounding") — and 12 rows below each hold a number x (to one decimal place) with its integer result under each of the five. The seed picks the 12 values from a fixed pool of 20 (from −3.5 to 6.5, including negatives, 0 and several exact halves) and lists them in ascending order; in rows where x is exactly a half the RN and RE columns have a darker background, since those are the only cases where the two can differ (for example 2.5: RN gives 3 and RE gives 2; 3.5: both give 4). A result of zero is always printed as "0", never with a minus sign. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only those five modes, and x with one decimal place; the pool of values is fixed, not arbitrary numbers; the dot-matrix text is hard to read at small sizes (the four characters of the x column exactly fill the cell width). Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`binaryaddition` adds binary addition, bringing the current catalog from 1043 to 1044 kinds (1045 gallery choices including plain). Each tile stacks three column additions from top to bottom, each adding two random 8-bit binary numbers: a row of small digits on top holds the carries (into positions 1 to 8), followed by addend A, addend B with a "+" sign, a rule, and at the bottom the 9-bit sum (one bit longer than the addends to hold the final carry), with a darker background on the sum row. Each small carry digit sits right above the column that receives it; the units column receives no carry, so it has none. Every column obeys the full-adder rule: A bit + B bit + carry in = sum bit + 2 × carry out. A and B in the three problems come from the seed (hash randomness); carry chains are not deliberately selected. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only 8-bit unsigned addition, with no negative numbers, overflow flag or decimal equivalent; carries are only smaller, lighter digits without a colour of their own (procedural materials only have a dark-to-light ramp). Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`abeliansandpile` adds an abelian sandpile, bringing the current catalog from 1044 to 1045 kinds (1046 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is the stable pile left after thousands of grains are dropped on the centre cell of an 81 × 81 grid: whenever a cell holds four or more grains it gives one grain to each of its four neighbours per four it holds, until every cell holds at most three; grains that leave the grid vanish. Each cell is painted in one of four brightnesses (darkest to lightest) for 0 to 3 grains. The seed picks the grain count from 4000, 5200 and 6400. The final pile of such a sandpile does not depend on the order of the topplings (the abelian property), so the result is deterministic and has the eightfold symmetry of the square. Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only a single source at the centre and only three grain counts, with no other initial configurations; there are no lines between cells, and the edges show slight stair-steps when the grid does not line up with pixels (81 cells do not map to a whole number of pixels). The pile covers only part of the tile; the rest is the 0-grain background. The sampler has to simulate the whole pile when it is first called and is a lookup afterwards; the preview script measured about 224 ms per run at 512² (Bun, not a formal benchmark). Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`percolationclusters` adds percolation clusters, bringing the current catalog from 1045 to 1046 kinds (1047 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a 48 × 48 site-percolation schematic: every cell is "open" independently with probability p, and open cells that touch along an edge form a cluster; the largest cluster gets the darkest brightness, other clusters of at least 8 cells a middle brightness and smaller ones a lighter brightness, with closed cells in the paper background. The seed picks p from 0.56, 0.5927 and 0.63 (0.5927 is the site-percolation threshold of the square lattice). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only square-lattice site percolation; clusters do not connect across the tile boundary (the grid is cut at the edges, so clusters of neighbouring tiles do not join and breaks show at the tile seams); there are no lines between cells, the sizes have only three levels, and brightness only encodes cluster size, no other physical quantity. The three values of p are fixed, and the threshold or whether a cluster spans the tile is not marked. Defaults are a 0.6 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`gcdtable` adds a GCD table, bringing the current catalog from 1046 to 1047 kinds (1048 gallery choices including plain). Each tile is a 12 × 12 table of greatest common divisors: the dark header row and header column each hold 12 consecutive integers whose starting values the seed picks from 1, 5, 13 and 25 (one for the rows and one for the columns, which may differ), and each body cell prints the greatest common divisor of its row number and column number. Coprime cells (divisor 1) are the lightest and the others darken as the divisor grows (brightness = 0.5 + 0.44 ÷ divisor). Metallic and mortar are 0.

This is an authored schematic: only the 12 × 12 range and only four starting values, with no prime factorisations or formulas; brightness only encodes the size of the divisor, not a classification. Two-digit numbers (such as 12 and 16) nearly fill the cell width. Defaults are a 0.5 m nominal tile, 0.004 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 0. Existing periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership are reused without new APIs or dependencies. Prefer 1024 or larger; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim three-backend or cross-browser verification.

`to92packagefaces` adds four rotated/jittered 2×2 TO-92 front schematics, bringing the current catalog from 885 to 886 kinds (887 gallery choices including plain). Matte-black D-shaped bodies have straight sides, flat bottoms and upper elliptical domes; three equally spaced parallel straight neutral-metal leads have broad root shoulders and narrow tails, with roots masked by the body. No exposed tab, mounting hole or markings are drawn. Full leads have M1; body/backing have M0, with C1 boundary blends. Authored normalized heights are backing .02 < lead .44 < body .68.

The dimension reference is the actually downloaded and raster-viewed [onsemi P2N2222A/D, January 2013 Rev. 7, PDF page 6](https://www.onsemi.com/pdf/datasheet/p2n2222a-d.pdf#page=6): embedded drawing 98ASB42022B, TO-92 (TO-226), CASE 29-11, Issue AM, dated 09 MAR 2007, drawing page 1 of 2, STRAIGHT LEAD BULK PACK view. Its A 4.45–5.20 mm, B 4.32–5.33 mm, G 1.15–1.39 mm and K ≥12.70 mm are dimension references only, not measurements of the artwork. The upper half ellipse, proportions, shoulders, color, roughness, AO and heights are authored simplifications, not exact CAD. No chemistry, plating, optical or electrical properties are inferred; no source artwork is shipped.

Defaults are a 0.08 m nominal tile, 0.0042 UV-relative relief (not metres of displacement) and metallic factor 1. Explicit `createMaterial({ metallic: 0 })` changes only the scalar factor, not the stored M mask. The TO-220 constructor-cached pose/grain and C1 role-replacement structure is reused, preserving periodic opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache APIs and texture ownership without new APIs or dependencies. Remove borrowed materials before destroying the texture-owning preset; manage caller-owned pools/caches separately. Prefer 512 for the three narrow tails; 32 is a coarse preview. This description does not claim runtime/browser verification.

`ftypefemalefaces` adds four 2×2 bare F-type female bulkhead front schematics, bringing the catalog from 883 to 884 kinds: six-flat nickel-plated metal hex bases, concentric raised round shoulders, CLOSED unkeyed raised circular lips, pale P.P. dielectric disks and tiny deeper dark opaque central female entries. Exterior hardware has M1; backing, P.P. and entries have M0. Authored normalized height order is backing .02 < entry .06 < nut .26 < shoulder .36 < P.P. .48 < lip .66. The entry is a height depression, not a painted dot; pale P.P. remains continuous around it. Unlike ST, there is no open keyway or bayonet stud, and no male pin or invented visible central metal contact ring.

The primary reference is the actually downloaded and raster-viewed historical [Amphenol Connex CAT-103 ©2001 catalog, PDF page 159 / printed page 158](https://docs.rs-online.com/1ab7/A700000006717073.pdf#page=159): the 222121 BULKHEAD ADAPTER JACK-TO-JACK photograph, drawing and model-specific Nickel / P.P. / 75Ω table. Generic Delrin/Teflon notes elsewhere do not override this model's P.P.; neither base-alloy chemistry nor exact contact material is inferred. The drawing's 11 HEX and 21.3 mm axial overall length are references, not measured front geometry; washer/nut side dimensions are not front height. The tiny actual entry cross-section is unresolved at source-photo resolution: its circular form here is a deliberate artist simplification, not exact CAD. Proportions, color, roughness, AO, layer heights, default 0.04 m tile and 0.0042 UV-relief factor are authored; .0042 is not metres or a calibrated depth. No thread helix/decorative thread turns, washer teeth, rear/cable/PCB, real holes, transparency or new mesh; no RF/75Ω, electrical, optical, mating or safety simulation/certification. Historical 75Ω identifies the part only. Source assets are not bundled; this extends the existing `pbr3d` gallery, not a new examples entry.

The independent 46-field preset (44 numeric fields and two palettes) uses ten C1 profiles: six smooth hex half-planes and four squared-radius circle profiles. Nested convex role replacement and 20 constructor-cached C/H/R/AO deltas avoid nonzero hard-min/max joins. Four four-column Float64 poses (128 B) plus 96² Float64 grain (73,728 B) retain 73,856 B typed-array payload, excluding JS object/closure overhead—not total RAM, FPS or a performance improvement claim. The rotation-independent owner bound, maxScale × nutInradius / (sqrt(3)/2) + jitter, is approximately .369958 < .5. Constructor-cached poses/grain leave no query allocation, loop, hash, trigonometry or square root; zero support and unused grain on full P.P./entry/backing are skipped. The second fractional wrap before owner indexing is retained for tiny negative UVs. Default metallic is 1; explicit `createMaterial({ metallic: 0 })` changes only the factor, not the stored hardware/nonmetal mask. Existing opaque four maps, optional height, tint/repeats and sync/Worker/cache APIs and ownership remain unchanged: remove borrowing materials before disposing the texture-owning preset, and manage caller-owned pools/caches separately. No new dependency or API. These are source/implementation descriptions, not full-suite, runtime, browser or built-distribution verification claims.

`rcafemalefaces` adds four 2×2 RCA/Cinch female-face schematics: broad round black insulating washers, smaller round metal shoulders, still smaller six-flat front barrel noses, dark annular insulators and deep round bores with simplified recessed conductive socket annuli and dark opaque floors. There are no BNC bayonet lugs, male center pins or large rear mounting nuts on the front. This is a connector-family variation, not new rendering or connector-simulation technology. The catalog then contained 879 kinds.

Appearance references actually read/viewed: the primary [REAN NYS367-0 drawing](https://www.rean-connectors.com/media/14879/download/NYS367-0.pdf?v=1), drawing 3.652.052 dated 2020-11-13, and the generic [NYS367 product PDF](https://www.rean-connectors.com/en/product/nys367-1.pdf). The drawing labels washer Ø15.5, barrel Ø8.3, front length 7.5, M8×0.75 and overall (29); AF12 describes the large rear nut, not a front measurement. Its inconsistent NYS373-x note is not evidence for a new variant, and the product PDF URL is not evidence of a NYS367-1 variant. Product photographs show the small front flats, wider washer and rear nut/lug; the PDF lists brass/gold housing, nut, lug and pin and PA+30%GF washer, not a specified inner dielectric resin. Neutral gray deliberately omits real gold optics; all shapes, positions, relief, palette, roughness and the 0.04 m nominal tile / 0.0042 UV relief are authored approximations, not measured CAD, tolerances or a hidden cross-section.

The independent 56-field preset/kernel combines six radial squared-distance C1 profiles with three smooth folded hex slabs and 27 cached channel deltas. Four four-column poses (128 B) and 96² Float64 grain (73,728 B) retain 73,856 typed-array payload bytes, not total RAM or FPS. Sampling uses no per-point allocation, hash, trigonometry, square root or per-side loop; `Math.sqrt(3) / 2` is evaluated only at construction. Default metallic 1 retains metal shoulders/barrels/socket contacts and nonmetallic washers/insulators/floors; explicit 0 changes the material factor, not the stored map. Existing opaque four maps, optional height, tint/repeats, synchronous/module Worker pool/cache paths and preset-owned texture cleanup remain unchanged, as do all previous 878 kinds. No new dependency or API entry point is added. Prefer 512; 32 is a coarse preview that need not resolve the small socket. No rear nut/lug/solder tails, actual threads or spring split, panel/PCB geometry, mesh, through-hole transparency, collision, electrical/audio/RF/fit/EMC/safety simulation or certification is provided.

`rj45femalefaces` adds four schematic RJ45-type 8P8C female faces: thin rectangular metal shields surround matte-black insulating liners; low main wells join narrower stepped latch extensions on one side, with single rows of eight isolated long metal fingers opposite. This differs from nine four-contact `rectangularsocketfaces` and USB-C's two-row 24-pad tongues, not merely a recolor or new technology category. The actually read [TE 1734795-6 product page](https://www.te.com/en/product-1734795-6.html) specifies eight positions/eight loaded contacts, no indicator, black PA9T GF30, gold/nickel-plated phosphor-bronze contacts and nickel-plated brass shells. Neutral gray omits gold-plating optics; the step, pad layout, dimensions, depths, colors, PBR roughness and 0.054 m nominal tile/0.0044 UV relief are authored approximations, not exact CAD or a reproduction of a manufacturer mechanical drawing.

The independent 55-field kernel caches fifteen channel deltas and five squared-distance C1 profiles (outer/liner/main/key/pin); main + key − main × key joins the wells without a max seam. Queries select one nearest contact candidate without a per-pad loop, arrays, hash, trigonometry or square root. Four four-column poses (128 B) plus 96² Float64 grain (73,728 B) retain 73,856 B typed payload, not total RAM or FPS. Default metallic 1 preserves M1 shields/fingers and M0 insulation/floors; explicit 0 changes the factor, not the stored map. Existing opaque four maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker pool/cache and texture ownership remain unchanged, as do the previous 877 kinds. No new dependency, LEDs, plug/wires, magnetics, PCB/solder tails or panel EMI tabs; no cavity mesh, Ethernet/PoE/Cat/speed/electrical/EMC/safety simulation or mating certification.

`usbcfemalefaces` adds four schematic USB-C female faces: rounded-flat metal mouths, recessed matte-dark wells, thin central insulating tongues and two rows of twelve metal pads per face (24 total), distinct from the existing nine four-contact `rectangularsocketfaces`, not a new technology category. The actually viewed [GCT USB4050 drawing](https://gct.co/files/drawings/usb4050.pdf), dated 2016-04-21, revision C (2023-06-29), is stamped OBSOLETE, not evidence of current availability or certification. It specifies black high-temperature plastic without a resin identification; neutral-gray metal omits actual gold-plating optics. Rounded-flat mouths are not exact capsules; side grounds/latches, stacked shells and PCB tails are omitted.

The independent kernel uses 50 preset fields, fifteen cached channel deltas, four squared-distance profiles and one nearest contact candidate. Four poses (128 B) plus 96² periodic grain (73,728 B) retain 73,856 B typed-array storage, not measured total RAM or FPS. Point sampling uses no arrays, hash, trigonometry or square root; old kernels gain no sampling work. Default metallic 1 preserves M1 mouths/pads and M0 insulation/floors; explicit 0 changes the factor, not the stored map. The 0.032 m nominal tile and 0.0045 UV relief are authored approximations, not calibrated mating dimensions. Opaque height/normal shading is not a cavity mesh, CAD, electrical/USB speed/PD/safety simulation or certification. Existing four maps, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and texture ownership remain unchanged; no new backend certification or runtime dependency is added.

`iecc6inlets` adds four schematic IEC C6 male inlet faces: flat black-PA6 rounded rectangular flanges surround one shared upper-single/lower-pair cloverleaf well, three solid round metal contact tips and two wide countersunk mounting depressions. Tips rise above the floor but remain below the flange; there is no raised C8 guard. The official PDF returned 403; pages 1/2 of the [RS-hosted original three-page SCHURTER 2561 datasheet](https://docs.rs-online.com/a592/0900766b814421a9.pdf), dated 2015-04-22, were downloaded, rendered and magnified to inspect its photograph/front elevation and C6, black PA6 and solid-turned-contact descriptions. The front drawing has a 37×19 mm/R4 flange, 28 mm mounting pitch and Ø3.2 bores/Ø6.4 countersinks; 18 mm is side-view depth, not front height. Flange, shared well, countersinks and opaque mounting-hole floors share one PA6 albedo, with height/AO shading. Only the three simplified visible tips are M1; no alloy/plating is inferred. Concentric contact bases, markings, rear terminals, shaft lengths and protective-earth sequencing are omitted. This is not C5 female sockets, C14 blades, geometric through-holes, mains simulation or IEC/safety certification.

The independent kernel uses 47 preset fields, seventeen constructor-cached channel deltas and five squared-profile coefficient groups. Four centers/scaled rotation bases retain 128 B plus 73,728 B of 96² periodic grain: 73,856 B typed storage, not total RAM or FPS. The owner bound hypot(.37,.19)+.016 < .431933 < .5 permits one floor per coordinate and one owner. Six profile candidates cover the rounded flange, countersink, bore, upper lobe, absolute-x lower lobe and nearest circular contact. A C1 union connects the upper/lower lobes; lower supports vanish around the fold, and all three contact supports vanish before nearest-center bisectors. Counter-annulus, well-bounding-box and outer-contact returns avoid unnecessary work. Queries allocate nothing and use no hash, trigonometry, square root, per-contact or neighbor loop. Before/after optimization, 24,576 signed samples/147,456 channel values and six five-map sets were exactly identical. Default metallic 1 preserves M1 tips/M0 polymer; explicit 0 only changes the factor. The .1 m nominal tile, .0045 UV normal relief and smooth cloverleaf/bowls are authored approximations, not calibrated CAD, contact depths or material measurements. Opaque maps, optional height, tint/repeats/Worker/cache and texture ownership retain existing contracts without new public entry points or runtime dependencies.

`iecc8inlets` adds four schematic IEC C8 male inlet faces: black PA6 rounded rectangular flanges carry raised capsule-shaped shrouds, semicircular well ends and symmetric rounded-V upper/lower waists forming one shared figure-eight cavity. Two solid round metal contact tips rise above each low floor but remain below the guard. The [SCHURTER 2572 page](https://www.schurter.com/en/datasheet/2572) was read for C8, PA6 and solid-contact descriptions. Official PDF/JPEG assets returned 403; pages 1/3 of the [TME-hosted original 2023 five-page datasheet](https://www.tme.eu/Document/3aea005c480575d6ca262e097188b992/6160.0008.pdf) were actually downloaded, rendered and magnified to inspect the photograph and front elevation. This is not two overlapping circular bores, a C7 female socket, three C14 blades or a polarized outline. Flange, shroud, waist and opaque floor share black-PA6 albedo with height/AO shading; only the two simplified visible tips are M1, without asserting alloy/plating. Concentric or toothed contact bases, small molded recesses, markings and rear terminals are omitted; no geometric through-hole, mains simulation or IEC/safety certification.

`din5femalefaces` adds four schematic standard five-pin DIN female faces: black PBT rounded rectangular flanges have three mounting openings (upper center and lower pair), closed raised circular mouths, keyed upper insert cutouts and lower structural webs. The independent kernel follows cached-pose, periodic-grain and compact-C1 conventions: 128B poses plus 73,728B of 96² grain retain 73,856B typed storage, not total RAM or FPS. An independent fixture segments raised footprints/M1 components, rejects diagonal coincidences using convex support lines, recovers four banks/pitch from observed centers, then traces five raw-height contours from between-land board edges and fits notch circles, without poses or nearest-row formulas. Crescent centroids can lie in empty notches, so fitted-contour rays find actual M1 cores for flatness/coplanarity, contrast and open M0 throats; all low backing must stay connected. All 49 legal variants pass 15 cases, including 3/5/6/8/9 contacts per side, 1/9 boards, rectangular outer lands, slightly offset centers, flush faces and no baked AO. All 32 faults are rejected by AssertionError; an additional closed-via control retains 112 metal regions but creates 113 background components, directly exercising openness. `radiolariandiscs` adds four authored radiolarian equatorial cutaways: four concentric ivory mineral rings enclose a central chamber and three annular rows of 6/12/18 chambers, 37 dark closed chambers per disc, with six smoothly tapering short marginal spines. Skeletons are raised, brighter and smoother than the low rough occluded dark backing; seeds vary placement, orientation, scale and microfinish. These are skeletal equatorial sections, not external porous sieve plates, fixed-count through-going `spiderweb` spokes, flush `orbicularjasper`/`thomsonite` pigment bands or lanceolate `diatomvalves` striae. The actually read [Haeckel Discoidea overview and Stylodictya entries](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Report_on_the_Radiolaria/Discoidea), [Plate 41 legends](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Report_on_the_Radiolaria/Plates5) and actually viewed Plate 41 distinguish figures 2/3/6/7 as rings alone/equatorial sections from complete sieve-covered faces. Four rings, 6/12/18 chambers and six short spines are simplified author choices, not an identified species, modern taxonomy, complete cell, calibrated microscopy or silica optics; no source image is copied or bundled.
