Ink and paper tones differ by 0.8367 (above 0.5), ink roughness exceeds paper roughness by 0.2433 (above 0.12), ink height exceeds paper height by 0.2919 relief units (above 0.15) and metallic violations are 0. Seeds 7 and 0xffffffff flip 0 of 1,276,900 ink-mask pixels, read the same bit strings and move the paper tone up to 0.07846/0.07882 (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, sweeps across every module boundary, the three bar-end rows and the corners where guard and normal bars meet, and curvature refinement: worst 0.00094 (below 0.04) and signed-UV period error at most 1.1e-16 (below 5e-10). The 512 map has ink fraction 0.272617 (0.61% from the reference), ink minus paper roughness 0.24202, AO 242-250, luma 31.2-230.6, maximum tilt² 0.58725 (0.1-3), sideways normal fractions at least 0.0997 and up/down fractions at least 0.002727 (the bar ends are thin); the 32 map keeps only sparse aliased ink (fraction 0.117188, tilt² 0.0034756, no decode asserted because a bar is narrower than a pixel). A regression control accepted 11 legal variants (9 preset variants: bluish-white and kraft paper palettes, paper roughness 0.4, paper 0.7 with ink 0.9, ink roughness 0.7, relief 0.008 and 0.02, bar edge half-widths 0.06 and 0.15, plus two source variants: a quintic edge profile and a finer paper grain) and rejected 19 genuine faults (4 preset faults: bars blurred by an edge half-width of 0.5, ink as rough as the paper, ink smoother than the paper, unresolved relief; 15 source faults: wrong check digit, L and G swapped in the left group, right group using L-codes, mirrored symbol, missing centre guard, guard bars as short as the others, bars too short, linear creased bar edges, inverted white bars on dark, seed ignored by the grain, seed moving the digits, metallic ink, debossed ink, ink barely darker than the paper, a 3-area quiet zone). Compatibility: all 841 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,548 samples, 141,288 channel comparisons) and eleven related kinds (`rule30`, `perfectmaze`, `sevensegment`, `ulamspiral`, `papertape`, `moorecurve`, `punchcard`, `dominoes`, `tactilepaving`, `sandpaper`, `embossedbraille`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (66 map sets, 330 buffers) are identical to the previous build; presets and tile sizes of the old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first 512 px check required all four signed normal directions above 0.005, but the thin bar ends tilt up and down over only 0.2727% of the pixels, so the check now asks 0.05 for the sideways pair and 0.001 for the up/down pair; the first 32 px check required an ink fraction above 0.1 and the measured 0.117188 was too close, so the bound was relaxed to 0.05; decoding at module centres cannot see blurred edges (a half-width of 0.5 still decodes exactly), so the grain-free edge width check was added after that fault was designed; a first draft of the fixture used toEqual, which the nested control harness does not support, and compares joined strings instead.

`embossedbraille` adds an authored embossed braille tile: a cream paper face of 7 × 4 braille cells whose smoothly raised round dots spell the letters a to z in order (w skipped, three blank cells at the end), the dots slightly smoother and a little darker than the flat paper. It is not `tactilepaving` (a regular grid of truncated-cone warning studs), `dominoes` (standard pips) or any other existing dotted preset, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for braille, tactile, emboss and raised dot found no braille preset (the braille hits are search-word lists in neighboring entries; tactile and emboss hit unrelated presets). The actually read Wikipedia Braille page (cells formed from six raised dots in two columns of three, 64 possible patterns including none, positions numbered top to bottom 1 to 3 on the left and 4 to 6 on the right, dot pattern 1-3-4 being the letter m; the first ten letters a-j use the upper four positions as ⠁⠃⠉⠙⠑⠋⠛⠓⠊⠚, k-t are the same with an added dot 3 as ⠅⠇⠍⠝⠕⠏⠟⠗⠎⠞, and the next decade u v x y z adds dots 3 and 6 with w left out) supplies the cells; the page gives no dot spacing, tile or rendering, so the 7 × 4 layout, the cell pitch of 2.4 and line pitch of 4.2 dot spacings (a 16.8 × 16.8 spacing square), the blank cells, the 0.3 spacing dome radius and every other value are authored: each raised dot is a dome with height profile (1 − s²)² of the squared normalized radius (zero slope at the rim, no square root) placed in a cell at columns ±0.5 and rows −1, 0, 1, the paper is 0.4 high and the dots reach 1.0, tone is 0.82 ± 0.1 grain less 0.12 on a dot with the 168,152,128 / 244,236,216 palette, roughness is 0.85 on the paper and 0.7 at a dot top, occlusion 0.97 falling by 0.1 and relief 0.03; these values are authored approximations, no paper material, dot spacing standard, embossing process or readability is claimed, and no image is copied or bundled. One UV tile represents 0.042 m (16.8 dot spacings of 2.5 mm, an authored typical spacing). The independent kernel stores the 28-cell code table and a 128² grain, 131,100 B of tables (16,384 Float64 values for the grain), not total RAM; each point reads one cell code, picks the nearest dot position and evaluates at most one dome, so no hypot, square root, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped and mortar stays 0. Seeds change only the paper grain while the raised dots stay fixed; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: the test builds the reference cells itself from the page (the a-j cells as literal characters, k-t as those with dot 3 added, u v x y z as a-e with dots 3 and 6 added, three blank cells), reads the sampler at each of the six dot positions of all 28 cells, and labels a 1000 × 1000 pixel-center raster (59.5 px per dot spacing) into connected dome components at the half-roughness contour. The page's own pattern 1-3-4 is checked to be m and the a-j cells to avoid dots 3 and 6. Every seed decodes abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvxyz followed by three blank cells with 0 mismatched cells and 80 raised dots, the raster has 80 dome components, none more than 0.03 dot spacing from a grid position (columns ±0.5, rows −1, 0, 1), and the mean footprint is 0.082922 spacing² against the predicted π(0.3)²(1 − √½) = 0.082814 (0.13% off; the test allows 5%). Dot/paper colors are 0.78089/0.86877 (0.7818/0.86865 and 0.78288/0.86871 at seeds 7 and 0xffffffff), roughness 0.73969/0.84855, heights 0.84125/0.40579 in relief units and metallic violations 0; seeds 7 and 0xffffffff flip 0 of 1,000,000 dot mask pixels, decode the same text and move paper tone up to 0.098682/0.099058 (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, lines at every cell border, cell center and dot column or row offset in both axes and curvature refinement; the worst value is 0.00004279 for all seeds (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error at most 2.83e-15 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has dot fraction 0.022972, dot/paper roughness 0.73883/0.84944, AO 222–247, luma 211.82–229.47 (range above 12), maximum tilt² 0.70622 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.0392–0.039742 (above 0.01); the 32 px map has no dot pixel at all (dot fraction 0, roughness 0.84995 everywhere, AO 241–247, tilt² 0.0055671 below 0.5). A regression control accepted 12 legal variants (10 preset variants: white and kraft paper palettes, paper roughness 0.75 with dot roughness 0.6, paper roughness 0.92, dot roughness 0.55, paper 0.9 with dot 0.72, relief 0.02/0.045 and dome radius 0.25/0.34, plus two source variants with softer dome occlusion and finer paper grain) and rejected 17 genuine faults (4 preset faults: dome radius 0.55 where neighboring dots merge, dome radius 0.08, dots as rough as the paper, unresolved relief; 13 source faults: m replaced by n, dots numbered by rows, rows reversed, columns mirrored, six cells per line, a narrow cell pitch, five lines, a linear dome profile, metallic dots, seed-ignored grain, seed-moved letters, debossed dots, dots lighter than the paper). Compatibility: all 840 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,520 samples, 141,120 channel comparisons) and ten related kinds (`rule30`, `perfectmaze`, `sevensegment`, `ulamspiral`, `papertape`, `moorecurve`, `punchcard`, `dominoes`, `tactilepaving`, `sandpaper`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (60 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: at 32 px every dot is smaller than a pixel and falls between pixel centers, so the 32 px check only validates the paper map and asserts no dot count; the dome mask is the half-roughness contour, so a raised-height check was added to reject debossed dots; the pixel dot threshold was first the constant 0.78, which classified a legal paper-roughness 0.75 variant entirely as dots, so it is derived from the preset roughness pair; the legal paper-roughness 0.75 variant first broke the 0.08 roughness-gap contract with the default dot roughness 0.7, so the variant carries dot roughness 0.6; the minimum-AO bound went from 235 to 240 because the legal softer-occlusion variant measures 235; and this kernel has no smoothstep, so the legal source variants are softer occlusion and finer grain rather than the quintic substitution used elsewhere.

`rule30` adds an authored Rule 30 cellular-automaton tile: a 33 × 33 cell tile showing 33 generations of Wolfram's elementary cellular automaton Rule 30 grown from a single black cell in the middle of the top row (time running down), the black cells raised glossy dark squares and the white cells lower, rougher pale squares, separated by recessed fine grooves. It is not `sierpinskicarpet`, `moorecurve`, `ulamspiral`, `perfectmaze` or any other existing geometric or number-derived preset, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for cellular automaton, Wolfram, rule 30, cone snail and pseudorandom found no preset (the only hits are search-word lists in neighboring entries). The actually read Wikipedia Rule 30 page (an elementary cellular automaton introduced by Stephen Wolfram in 1983, a Class III rule with aperiodic, chaotic behaviour; the rule set 111→0, 110→0, 101→0, 100→1, 011→1, 010→1, 001→1, 000→0, that is left xor (center or right), named because 00011110 in binary is 30; from a single cell with state 1 among 0s, time running down, it shows frequent white triangles and a well-defined striped pattern on the left side; the numbers of black cells per generation are 1, 3, 3, 6, 4, 9, 5, 12, 7, 12, 11, 14, 12, 19, 13, 22, 15, 19, ...; a similar pattern appears on the shell of the cone snail Conus textile) supplies the rule and the counts; the page gives no tile, grid size, boundary or appearance, so the 33 × 33 grid (cells outside the array are 0, so generations beyond the sixteenth are clipped by the side edges) and every dimension are authored: each cell is a square of half size 0.42 cell with 0.05 cell shoulders (the product of two smooth intervals, 0.79 cell wide at the half-coverage contour), the black cells are raised to 0.9 with tone 0.1 and roughness 0.3, the white cells sit at 0.4 with tone 0.7 ± 0.08 grain and roughness 0.8, the grooves are at 0.3 with tone 0.35, the palette is 20,22,30 / 226,222,208, occlusion is 0.97 falling by 0.3 in the grooves and relief is 0.03; these values are authored approximations, no material, size standard or randomness property is claimed, and no image is copied or bundled. One UV tile represents 0.66 m (33 cells of 2 cm). The independent kernel stores a 1,089-byte cell table and a 128² grain, 132,161 B of tables (16,384 Float64 values for the grain), not total RAM; the automaton runs only while the sampler is created, and each point reads one cell byte and evaluates two intervals, so no hypot, square root, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped and mortar stays 0. Seeds change only the white-cell grain while the pattern stays fixed; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: the test grows Rule 30 itself from the rule table printed on the page (one 1 in the middle of the top row, 0 outside the array), reads the sampler at the center of every one of the 1,089 cells, and labels a 1000 × 1000 pixel-center raster (30.3 px per cell, so the pixel alignment differs from cell to cell) into connected black-cell components. Every seed reads all 1,089 cells equal to the independent automaton (0 mismatches) with 445 black cells, the black-cell counts of the first 17 generations are 1, 3, 3, 6, 4, 9, 5, 12, 7, 12, 11, 14, 12, 19, 13, 22, 15 exactly as printed on the page (later generations are clipped by the 33 columns), and 402 cells differ from their mirror image so the triangle is not symmetric (a mirror image would be Rule 86). The raster has 445 black components in 445 different cells, none more than 0.03 cell from its cell center, with a mean area of 0.62356 cell² against the predicted 0.6241 for the half-coverage square (0.09% off; the test allows 3%). Black/non-black colors are 0.10569/0.54131 (0.10569/0.54113 and 0.10569/0.54109 at seeds 7 and 0xffffffff; the non-black average includes the grooves), roughness 0.31137/0.79596, heights 0.88635/0.35437 in relief units and metallic violations 0; seeds 7 and 0xffffffff flip 0 of 1,000,000 mask pixels, keep 0 mismatches and move white-cell tone up to 0.078553/0.078962 (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, lines at every cell border, cell center and tile edge offset in both axes, diagonals through every cell across the tile corners and curvature refinement; the worst value is 0.00064474 for all seeds (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error at most 4.44e-16 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has black fraction 0.25616, black/white roughness 0.31457/0.79631, AO 171–247, luma 42.221–177.78 (range above 80, darkest below 70), maximum tilt² 0.98562 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.13696–0.13888 (above 0.01); the 32 px map keeps black fraction 0.22168, roughness 0.33606/0.79925, the same AO range, luma 42.221–177.99, tilt² 0.10719 (below 0.5) and signed fractions 0.13184–0.14063. A regression control accepted 13 legal variants (12 preset variants: blue-grey and sepia tile palettes, white-cell roughness 0.65/0.9, black-cell roughness 0.15/0.45, relief 0.02/0.045, half size 0.38/0.45 and shoulder 0.03/0.08, plus a quintic-smoothstep source variant) and rejected 21 genuine faults (5 preset faults: half size 0.58 that its cell cuts off, half size 0.1, no color range, black cells as rough as white cells, unresolved relief; 16 source faults: Rule 90, the mirror image Rule 86, the complement Rule 135, a reversed neighbour bit order, a seed cell off center, a wrap-around boundary, time running upward, linear edges, metallic black cells, seed-ignored grain, a seed-moved starting cell, grout gray blended into the black cells, black cells not raised, black and white swapped, 31 columns, creased tile corners). Compatibility: all 839 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,492 samples, 140,952 channel comparisons) and ten related kinds (`perfectmaze`, `sevensegment`, `ulamspiral`, `papertape`, `moorecurve`, `sierpinskicarpet`, `chladnifigure`, `punchcard`, `dominoes`, `crackleglaze`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (60 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first raster used 32 px per cell, so every tile was counted at the same pixel alignment (26 pixel centers across a 25.28 px tile) and the mean area read 5.15% high, so the raster became 1000 × 1000 (the ratio is now 0.99914) and the 3% tolerance was kept; the centroid tolerance went from 0.02 to 0.03 cell (about one pixel) because the legal shoulder 0.08 variant measured 0.0209 and the default 0.0196 sat at the old bound; a grout-gray fault was accepted because the luma range did not change and then rejected only marginally (darkest luma 92 against 90), so the darkest-luma bound became 70 (the default measures 42.221 and every legal palette variant passes); and the nested test harness has no toEqual, so the generation counts are compared as joined strings.

`perfectmaze` adds an authored seeded perfect-maze tile: an 8 × 8 cell maze whose corridors are recessed, dark, smoother channels between pale, rougher raised walls, the 64 cells joined by 63 links so that every cell is reachable and no loop exists, and a different maze for every seed. It is not `moorecurve` (one deterministic closed groove loop without branches), `braincoral` (natural labyrinthine ridges), `guilloche`, `greekkey`, `truchettiles`, `hextruchettiles`, `circuitboard` or the other existing groove and tiled presets, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for maze, labyrinth, spanning tree and backtrack found no maze preset (the labyrinth hits are `braincoral`'s natural ridges and `moorecurve`'s own wording). The actually read Wikipedia Maze generation algorithm page (a maze can be treated as a connected graph whose nodes are cells and whose edges are wall sites, and generation is often approached as a random spanning tree so that no region is unreachable and no loop confuses a solver; randomized depth-first search, the recursive backtracker, starts from a cell, moves to a random unvisited neighbour while removing the wall between them, backtracks at dead ends, and gives a low branching factor with many long corridors) supplies the construction; the 8 × 8 size, the xorshift generator seeded from the seed, the neighbour order (east, south, west, north), the corridor geometry and every dimension are authored: each cell joins its linked neighbours with a horizontal and a vertical band, each the product of two smooth intervals with half width 0.25 cell and shoulder 0.05 cell (so the half-coverage corridor is 0.45 cell wide, 3.6 cm at 8 cm cells), the bands run straight through the cell border toward a linked neighbour so cells join C1, the floor is the union 1 − (1 − horizontal)(1 − vertical), and no link crosses the tile border so every repeated tile holds its own maze. The floor is recessed 0.45 below the 0.8 wall; wall tone is 0.8 ± 0.1 grain and floor tone 0.2 with the 48,42,36 / 214,204,184 palette, roughness 0.55 on the walls and 0.85 in the corridors, occlusion 0.97 falling to 0.6 in the corridors and relief 0.03; these values are authored approximations, no material, size standard or difficulty is claimed, and no image is copied or bundled. One UV tile represents 0.64 m (8 × 8 cells of 8 cm). The independent kernel stores 64 link bytes and a 128² grain, 131,264 B of tables including the 128 scratch bytes of the generator (16,384 Float64 values for the grain), not total RAM; the depth-first search runs only while the sampler is created, and each point reads one link byte and evaluates four intervals, so no hypot, square root, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped and mortar stays 0. Seeds change the maze and the wall grain; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: the test reads the maze the way a walker sees it, one sampler read at each of the 64 cell centers and at each of the 112 shared edge midpoints (a read is open when it is recessed floor), builds the graph and checks it with a union-find and two breadth-first searches, and labels a 1024 × 1024 pixel-center raster (128 px per cell) into connected floor and wall components. Every seed gives 64 open cell centers, 63 links, 0 cycles, 64 cells reached from cell (0,0), and 0 open reads at the 16 tile-border midpoints; seeds 7 and 0xffffffff differ from seed 0 in 60 and 54 of the 112 possible links, re-creating a sampler for the same seed gives the same links, and the depth-first signature is 6/8/7 junction cells, 8/10/9 dead ends and a longest route of 50/47/41 steps at seeds 0/7/0xffffffff. The raster has 1 floor component and 1 wall component per seed, and the floor fraction is 0.45614/0.45615/0.45615 against 0.44613 predicted from the half-coverage corridor width (0.45 cell: 64 center squares plus 63 arms; 2.2% above because the unions round the inner corners, the test allows 5%). Floor/wall colors are 0.21505/0.84209 (0.21508/0.84253 and 0.21509/0.84274 at seeds 7 and 0xffffffff), roughness 0.84305/0.55353, occlusion 0.60858/0.96565 and metallic violations 0; the always-wall strip at u = 0.01 moves tone up to 0.066155/0.067177 between seeds (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, lines at every cell border, cell center and corridor edge offset in both axes, diagonals through every cell across the inner corners and curvature refinement; the worst value is 0.00002956/0.00002861/0.00002956 (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error at most 1.1e-14 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has floor fraction 0.44834, floor/wall roughness 0.84634/0.55474, AO 153–247, luma 74.911–188.54 (range above 80), maximum tilt² 0.90036 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.037117–0.045292 (above 0.01); the 32 px map keeps floor fraction 0.49609, roughness 0.85098/0.54902, the same AO and luma range, tilt² 0.086398 (below 0.5) and signed fractions 0.22266–0.27734. A regression control accepted 13 legal variants (12 preset variants: cool stone and warm sandstone palettes, wall roughness 0.4/0.6, floor roughness 0.8/0.95, relief 0.02/0.045, half width 0.2/0.3 and shoulder 0.03/0.07, plus a quintic-smoothstep source variant) and rejected 20 genuine faults (5 preset faults: half width 0.5 that makes the walls vanish, half width 0.02 that makes the corridors vanish, no color range, floor as rough as the walls, unresolved relief; 15 source faults: loops added to the tree, one link removed, an arm through the tile border, a seed-ignoring maze, Prim-like growth instead of depth first, linear edges, a metallic floor, seed-ignored grain, grout gray blended into the floor, a floor that is not recessed, a floor lighter than the walls, a 7 × 7 grid, creased junction corners, arms that stop short of the border, one-sided links). Compatibility: all 838 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,464 samples, 140,784 channel comparisons) and ten related kinds (`sevensegment`, `ulamspiral`, `papertape`, `moorecurve`, `sierpinskicarpet`, `chladnifigure`, `punchcard`, `dominoes`, `truchettiles`, `hextruchettiles`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (60 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first fixture accepted a Prim-like growth fault because its depth-first bounds (fewer than 20 junctions, diameter above 20) were too loose, so they were measured (Prim-like growth on eight seeds gave 13–21 junctions, 16–24 dead ends and diameter 16–26; depth-first on twelve seeds gave 4–9, 6–11 and 41–53) and tightened to fewer than 12 junctions, fewer than 14 dead ends and a diameter above 33, which is a statistical signature on three seeds and not a proof of the algorithm; a seed-ignored-grain fault was accepted because seeds also move the maze, so the always-wall strip probe was added; a creased-junction fault (the maximum of the two bands) was accepted because no checked line crossed its crease, so diagonals through every cell were added and it is now rejected (163.10 against 0.04); the floor fraction tolerance went from 3% to 5% after the measured 2.2% surplus from the rounded inner corners; and the AO bound went from 150 to 170 because the corridor occlusion is 0.6 (153).

`sevensegment` adds an authored seven-segment display panel tile: a dark, glossy-faced non-metallic panel in a 5 × 2 grid of digit cells showing 0123456789, the lit segments bright amber chamfered bars and the unlit segments dim ghosts, all recessed below the face. It is not `chipresistors` (a white 103 marking drawn as continuous strokes on component bodies) or any other existing preset that renders characters, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for seven-segment, segment display, digit and calculator found no preset that draws digits as segments. The actually read Wikipedia Segment display page (a seven-segment display shows digits and some letters; its segments are referred to by the letters a to g, with an optional decimal point; a byte can hold the whole state, and in the popular gfedcba encoding 0x06 lights segments c and b to display a 1; the most common decimal patterns are 0 abcdef, 1 bc, 2 abdeg, 3 abcdg, 4 bcfg, 5 acdfg, 6 acdefg, 7 abc, 8 abcdefg and 9 abcdfg, with alternatives such as a 7 with a tail) supplies the digit patterns; the fetched text gives no segment positions, proportions or appearance, so the layout (a top, b upper right, c lower right, d bottom, e lower left, f upper left, g middle, y running down in UV), the 5 × 2 grid in reading order and every dimension are authored: each bar has half length 0.3 and half width 0.06 of a digit cell width with 45° chamfered ends, centered at (0, ±0.8), (±0.34, ±0.4) and (0, 0) in a cell that is 1 wide and 2.5 tall, and each bar is the product of four smooth half-plane masks (two sides, two chamfers, the sign along the bar folded) so the pointed tips stay C1. The bars are recessed 0.1 below the 0.6 face; tone is 0.05 ± 0.02 grain on the panel, +0.15 on the unlit ghosts and +0.75 on the lit bars with the 14,10,10 / 255,150,40 palette, roughness 0.45 on the panel and 0.2 on the lit bars, occlusion 0.97 falling by 0.25 on the ghosts and relief 0.03; these values are authored approximations, no LED, LCD, emission or standard is modeled or claimed, and no image is copied or bundled. One UV tile represents 0.2 m (a 5 × 2 grid of cells 4 cm wide and 10 cm tall). The independent kernel stores a 128² grain, 131,072 B of table (16,384 Float64 values), not total RAM; each point folds one pattern code, skips bars that are out of reach and evaluates at most a few smooth masks, so no hypot, square root, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped and mortar stays 0. Seeds change only the panel grain while the digits stay fixed; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency, emission or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: the test reads the display the way a person does, one sampler read at each of the seven segment slots of each of the ten digit cells and matches the lit set against the page's digit patterns, then labels a 1000 × 1000 pixel-center raster (200 px per cell width) into connected bar components. Every seed decodes the text 0123456789 (49 lit slots and 21 unlit ghost slots, the gfedcba bytes of 1 and 8 being 0x06 and 0x7f), the raster has 70 bar components on 70 distinct slots, 49 of them lit on 49 distinct slots, each within 2e-16 digit widths of its slot center, the mean bar area is 0.0523 digit-width² against the predicted 0.052172 for the half-coverage contour (the nominal 4Lw − 2w² = 0.07735 less an inset of half the shoulder; 0.25% off, the test allows 3%) and the lit fraction is 0.10251 against 0.10226 predicted. Lit/panel colors are 0.92057/0.06464 (0.9205/0.064597 and 0.92046/0.064662 at seeds 7 and 0xffffffff), ghost tones 0.20083–0.21909 at seed 0, roughness 0.21098/0.4488 and metallic violations 0; seeds 7 and 0xffffffff flip 0 of 1,000,000 lit mask pixels, decode the same text and move panel tone up to 0.01973/0.019802 (above 0.005). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, lines at every cell border, cell center and segment slot offset in both axes and curvature refinement; the worst value is 0.00006008/0.00006008/0.00006104 (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error at most 1.25e-16 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has lit fraction 0.10221, lit/panel roughness 0.21044/0.44977, lit-bar chroma 0.77819 (above 0.3), AO 184–247, luma 18.552–159.96 (range above 80), maximum tilt² 0.47296 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.033173–0.036057 (above 0.01); the 32 px map keeps lit fraction 0.046875, roughness 0.2049/0.44958, chroma 0.79379, the same AO and luma range, tilt² 0.0026144 (below 0.5) and signed fractions 0.023438–0.03125. A regression control accepted 15 legal variants (14 preset variants: green and red LED palettes, panel roughness 0.35/0.55, lit roughness 0.1/0.3, relief 0.02/0.04, bar half length 0.27/0.31, half width 0.05/0.07 and shoulder 0.01/0.03, plus a quintic-smoothstep source variant) and rejected 23 genuine faults (6 preset faults: half width 0.3 that merges bars, half width 0.01, half length 0.45 where bars collide, no color range, lit bars as rough as the panel, unresolved relief; 17 source faults: an alternate 7 with a tail, an 8 without the middle bar, swapped left segments, digits 0 and 1 swapped, four columns, no unlit ghosts, every bar lit, linear edges, creased pointed tips, metallic lit bars, seed-ignored grain, seed-moved digits, grout gray blended into the bars, lit bars not brighter, bars not recessed, upside-down digits, wrong digit aspect). Compatibility: all 837 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,436 samples, 140,616 channel comparisons) and eleven related kinds (`ulamspiral`, `papertape`, `moorecurve`, `sierpinskicarpet`, `chladnifigure`, `punchcard`, `dominoes`, `tactilepaving`, `cardboardedge`, `orbicularjasper`, `staurolite`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (66 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first pointed-tip form measured the offset across the bar with an absolute value in both chamfer planes, which creases the bar axis near each tip, and the new fixture's C1 grid caught it (worst 476.12 against 0.04 in color at grid points), so each bar became a product of four half-plane masks and the creased form stays a rejected control; the first area prediction used the nominal bar and was 20.7% off because the half-coverage contour sits half a shoulder inside it, so the prediction was corrected rather than the tolerance loosened; relief 0.01 gave a 512 px maximum tilt² of 0.093, below the 0.1 bound, so relief became 0.03 (0.473); at 32 px the bars are under one pixel thick and the lit fraction 0.0469 is below the 0.05 bound used elsewhere, so the lower bound became 0.02 for both sizes; the legal shoulder 0.03 variant flipped 36 of 1,000,000 lit mask pixels between seeds because the mask was thresholded on the grain-carrying color, so the mask now uses the grain-free roughness midpoint and the digits are still decoded from color; and three control results needed correction: the first half-width 0.16 fault was accepted because the chamfers keep even fat bars apart (a legal extreme, replaced by 0.3 that merges bars into 50 components), the first grout-gray fault was accepted because the luma range did not change, which added the lit-bar chroma check (0.0696 for the fault against the 0.3 limit), and an excessive-relief fault of 0.5 was accepted because normals saturate (maximum tilt² 0.914 at relief 0.1 and 1.004 at 0.5), so no excessive-relief rejection is claimed for this kind.

`ulamspiral` adds an authored Ulam prime spiral tile: the integers 1-225 written in a 15 × 15 cell square spiral with 1 at the center, the cells holding primes marked by glossy dark raised domes on a pale, rougher non-metallic slab cut by recessed cell grooves. It is not `tactilepaving` (a regular grid of truncated-cone warning studs), `dominoes` (standard 0-6 pip layouts) or any other existing dotted or perforated preset, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for ulam, prime, spiral and number theory found no preset whose pattern comes from numbers (the existing spiral hits are curled forms such as fiddleheads, cinnamon bark coils and palmiers). The actually read Wikipedia Ulam spiral page (devised by Stanisław Ulam in 1963 and popularized by Martin Gardner; the positive integers are written in a square spiral, most often with 1 at the center, and the primes are marked; lines in the spiral correspond to quadratic polynomials 4n² + bn + c, and since all numbers on a diagonal line are odd or all are even, all primes other than 2 lie in alternate diagonals) supplies the construction; the fetched text does not describe the figure's orientation, so the direction (2 to the right of 1, then counterclockwise, rows running downward in UV), the 15 × 15 size and every dimension are authored: the cell at offset (x, y) from the center is numbered by ring r = max(|x|, |y|) from (2r + 1)² and the side it lies on, and a 226-entry sieve marks the primes (48 of 225). Each marked cell holds one dome of radius 0.38 cell with height profile (1 − s²)² of the squared normalized radius (zero slope at the rim, no square root), the slab is 0.3 high with grooves 0.12 deep and a half width of 0.05 cell at every cell border (a product union of two smooth one-dimensional boxes so cell corners join C1), domes reach 0.9, slab tone is 0.74 ± 0.12 grain minus 0.3 in the grooves and 0.09 on the domes with the 30,38,58 / 234,226,206 palette, roughness 0.8 on the slab and 0.3 on the domes, occlusion 0.97 falling to 0.65 in the grooves and relief 0.02; these values are authored approximations, no material, standard, product or mathematical certification is claimed, and no image is copied or bundled. One UV tile represents 0.375 m (15 cells of 2.5 cm). The independent kernel stores a 226-byte sieve, 225 prime flags and a 128² grain, 131,523 B of tables (16,384 Float64 values for the grain), not total RAM; the spiral numbering and sieve run only while the sampler is created, and each point reads one flag and evaluates two boxes plus at most one dome, so no hypot, square root, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped and mortar stays 0. Seeds change only the slab grain while the marked primes stay fixed; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: the test walks the integers 1-225 itself in a counterclockwise square spiral from 1 at the center, tests each by trial division, and reads the sampler at the center of every cell, then labels a 1050 × 1050 pixel-center raster (70 px per cell) into connected dome components. Every seed marks exactly the 48 prime cells (0 mismatches over 225 cells, one mark on 2, none on 1, and all 47 odd primes on cells with x + y even, the alternate diagonals), the raster has 48 dome components in 48 different cells, none more than 0.02 cell from its cell center, and the mean footprint is 0.22776 cell² against the predicted 0.5π(0.38)² = 0.22682 (0.4% off; the test allows 3%; the footprint is where the profile exceeds a quarter, s < 0.7071). Dome/slab colors are 0.09/0.74523 (0.09/0.74537 and 0.09/0.74549 at seeds 7 and 0xffffffff), roughness 0.3/0.78315, groove/slab heights 0.18/0.3 (relief units) and occlusion 0.65/0.97 with metallic violations 0; seeds 7 and 0xffffffff flip 0 of 1,102,500 dome mask pixels, keep the same marked primes and move slab tone up to 0.11787/0.11904 (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, lines at every cell border and cell center in both axes and curvature refinement; the worst value is 0.00003052/0.00003147/0.00003147 (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error at most 7.77e-16 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has dome fraction 0.080654, dome/slab roughness 0.32033/0.79836, AO 166–247, luma 54.667–199.26 (range above 80), maximum tilt² 0.34931 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.11023–0.11036 (above 0.01); the 32 px map keeps dome fraction 0.082031, roughness 0.32997/0.79904, AO 170–247, luma 54.667–199.98, tilt² 0.034725 (below 0.5) and signed fractions 0.044922–0.047852. A regression control accepted 13 legal variants (12 preset variants: blue-grey and warm slab palettes, slab roughness 0.65/0.9, dome roughness 0.2/0.45, relief 0.012/0.03, dome radius 0.33/0.42 and groove half width 0.03/0.07, plus a quintic-smoothstep source variant) and rejected 23 genuine faults (6 preset faults: dome radius 0.9 that its cell cuts off, dome radius 0.15, no color range, domes as rough as the slab, unresolved or extreme relief; 17 source faults: clockwise spiral, 1 not at the center, 1 counted as prime, composites marked, 2 skipped, an incomplete sieve, a 13 × 13 grid, seed-moved primes, a linear dome profile, metallic domes, no cell grooves, creased groove corners, seed-ignored grain, grout gray blended into the domes, domes not raised, light domes, swapped grid axes). Compatibility: all 836 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,408 samples, 140,448 channel comparisons) and eleven related kinds (`papertape`, `moorecurve`, `sierpinskicarpet`, `chladnifigure`, `punchcard`, `dominoes`, `tactilepaving`, `cardboardedge`, `orbicularjasper`, `staurolite`, `kisrhombilletiles`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (66 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first groove took the minimum of the four border distances, which creases the cell-corner diagonals, and the new fixture's C1 grid caught it (worst 143.44 against 0.04 in occlusion at pixel-grid point (0.3984, 0.3984)), so the groove became a product union of two smooth boxes, and the creased variant stays a rejected control; the first relief 0.012 left the 512 px maximum tilt² at 0.162, barely above the 0.1 bound, so relief became 0.02 (0.349); the generic code reads the preset field named `roughness` for every kind, so the first `slabRoughness` field failed the typecheck and was renamed, and ESLint's prefer-const finding on a Set was fixed, after which the targeted run was repeated; the dome-fraction lower bound went from 0.04 to 0.02 because the legal dome radius 0.33 variant measures 0.0366, while the separate footprint test still pins the size; and the first radius fault 0.7 was rejected only marginally (3.85% area error against 3%), so it was replaced by 0.9 (44 components instead of 48).

`papertape` adds an authored punched paper tape tile: one inch of eight-level tape (ten 0.1 inch character rows by nine hole tracks) in a pale non-metallic paper that encodes the ASCII text PAPER TAPE in punched holes, with a row of smaller sprocket holes nearer one edge and dark backing visible through every hole, the holes rougher, darker and with lower occlusion. It is not `punchcard` (2 × 4 cream cards with 20 × 8 rectangular punches) or any other existing perforated or dotted preset, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for punched tape, braille, morse, barcode, QR, seven-segment, dot-matrix, ulam, cellular automaton, piano roll and music box found no preset that encodes data in its pattern. The actually read Wikipedia Punched tape page (data are the presence or absence of holes in rows across the tape; a row of smaller sprocket holes is always punched, slightly closer to one edge so that it divides the tape into unequal widths with the narrower side holding the least significant bits; a common 1 inch wide tape for six or more bits, hole spacing 0.1 inch in both directions, data holes 0.072 inch (1.8 mm) and sprocket holes 0.046 inch (1.2 mm) in diameter; its example of binary 01011010 as ASCII Z) supplies the geometry; the fetched text gives no message, track assignment or parity convention, so these are authored: a hole is a 1 bit, the sprocket row is track 4 of nine (0.4 of the tape width from the narrow edge, 0.6 from the other), tracks 1-3 hold bits 0-2 and tracks 5-9 hold bits 3-7, and bit 7 is the even-parity bit, so every character row has an even number of data holes. The kernel builds each row byte from the ASCII code of PAPER TAPE plus that parity bit; the holes are discs of radius 0.36 (data) and 0.23 (sprocket) row pitches with a 0.05 smooth shoulder (the page's 0.072 and 0.046 inch diameters over the 0.1 inch pitch), the paper is 0.7 high and the backing 0.1 (a 0.6 drop), a tape-edge seam line is 0.012 of the tile wide and 0.15 deep, paper tone is 0.72 ± 0.14 grain with the 58,48,38 / 236,224,196 palette, roughness is 0.75 on the paper and 0.92 in the holes, occlusion falls from 0.97 to 0.30 in the holes and relief is 0.008; these values are authored approximations, the 0.1 mm tape thickness, translucency, oiled or chadless paper, tape reels and punching tolerances are not modeled, and no standard, device or product is claimed and no image is copied or bundled. One UV tile represents 0.0254 m (one inch of tape, ten character rows). The independent kernel stores 10 row bytes and a 128² grain, 131,082 B of tables (16,384 Float64 values for the grain), not total RAM; each point reads one row byte, selects the nearest track and evaluates at most one disc with one square root, so no hypot, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped and mortar stays 0. Seeds change only the paper fibres while the punched text stays fixed; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: the tape is read the way a reader would, one sampler read at each of the nine track centers of every one of the ten character rows, and then a 1000 × 1000 pixel-center raster (100 px per 0.1 inch pitch) is labeled into connected hole components. Every seed decodes the bit rows into the text PAPER TAPE (bits 0-6 as ASCII) with 0 rows of odd parity, 10 sprocket holes (one per row) and 28 data holes, so the raster has 38 hole components, none of them more than 0.02 pitch from a track center, 10 on track 4 and 28 elsewhere. Mean hole areas are 0.1304 pitch² for the sprocket holes and 0.3512 for the data holes against the predicted π(R − 0.025)² = 0.13203 and 0.35257 (1.2% and 0.4% off; the test allows 3%), their ratio is 2.69 (above 1.8), and every sprocket hole centroid sits at track 4.0000 (0.4 of the tape width from the narrow edge, not at the center). Hole/paper colors are 0.0993/0.7834 (0.0993/0.7836 and 0.0993/0.784 at seeds 7 and 0xffffffff), roughness 0.9154/0.7507, occlusion 0.3182/0.9645 and metallic violations 0; the first raster row (the tape edge seam) is 0.2423/0.2519/0.2543 darker than a paper row inside the tape (above 0.05), and the hole fraction is 0.11138 against the predicted 0.11192 ((28 × 0.35257 + 10 × 0.13203) per 100 pitch²). Seeds 7 and 0xffffffff flip 0 of 1,000,000 hole mask pixels, decode the same text and move paper tone up to 0.1393/0.138 (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, lines at every row and track boundary and center in both axes and curvature refinement; the worst value is 0.000041078/0.000041633/0.000042744 (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error at most 1.58e-14 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has hole fraction 0.10985, hole/paper roughness 0.91772/0.74991, AO 76–247, luma 63.3318–199.746 (range above 80), maximum tilt² 0.62945 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.02205–0.03191 (above 0.01); the 32 px map keeps hole fraction 0.10352, roughness 0.92053/0.74965, the same AO and luma range, tilt² 0.011103 (below 0.5) and signed fractions 0.09473–0.09668. A regression control accepted 15 legal variants (14 preset variants: blue and yellow tape palettes, paper roughness 0.6/0.8, relief 0.005/0.012, data hole radius 0.33/0.39, sprocket hole radius 0.2/0.26, shoulder 0.03/0.08 and edge seam width 0.008/0.02, plus a quintic-smoothstep source variant) and rejected 21 genuine faults (7 preset faults: data hole radius 0.6 that merges neighboring holes, data hole radius 0.15, sprocket holes as large as data holes, no color range, paper as smooth as the holes, unresolved or extreme relief; 14 source faults: reversed bit order, odd parity, a different text, no sprocket holes, sprocket holes on the wrong track, wrong row pitch, seed-shifted punched code, linear hole edges, metallic backing, holes not recessed, no tape edge seam, seed-ignored paper fibres, grout gray blended into the holes, light backing in the holes). Compatibility: all 835 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,380 samples, 140,280 channel comparisons) and eleven related kinds (`moorecurve`, `sierpinskicarpet`, `chladnifigure`, `punchcard`, `cardboardedge`, `orbicularjasper`, `staurolite`, `starfish`, `halite`, `kisrhombilletiles`, `waffleweave`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (66 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first legal paper-roughness 0.85 variant left the hole/paper roughness gap at 0.0678 (below the 0.1 bound) and coincides with the 0.85 hole/paper classification threshold of the pixel check, so it was replaced by 0.8 (the contract is that holes are at least 0.1 rougher than the paper); the first data-radius fault 0.5 was accepted because the 0.475 effective radius still leaves the holes separate and decodable, so it was a legal extreme and was replaced by 0.6, which merges neighbors (25 components instead of 38); and ESLint flagged an unused text constant in the new fixture, which now feeds the decode assertion, after which the targeted run was repeated.

`moorecurve` adds an authored Moore-curve groove tile: one closed, continuous dark groove looping through every cell of an 8 × 8 cell tile in a pale non-metallic face, the groove recessed, rougher and darker. It is not `braincoral` (natural labyrinthine ridges), `guilloche`, `greekkey`, `truchettiles`, `hextruchettiles`, `circuitboard` or the other existing groove, maze and tiled presets, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for hilbert, peano, moore and space-filling found no preset with this curve (the existing meander and labyrinth hits are natural ridges and chains). The actually read Wikipedia Moore curve page (a continuous fractal space-filling curve, the loop version of the Hilbert curve, which may be thought of as the union of four copies of Hilbert curves combined so that the endpoints coincide) supplies the idea; the fetched text did not include its L-system rules, so the discrete construction is authored and numerically checked here: an order-2 Hilbert curve on a 4 × 4 block (iterative bit-conversion from curve index to cell, starting at its corner (0,0) and ending at (3,0) with adjacent steps) is rotated into the four quadrants (counter-clockwise bottom-left and top-left, clockwise top-right and bottom-right, entering and leaving at the vertical midline) so that the 64 cells form one Hamiltonian cycle whose closing step is also a neighbor step. The groove is the union of a central square and an arm toward each linked neighbor, each a product of smooth one-dimensional boxes (half width 0.17 cell, shoulder 0.04 cell, so the width is 0.3 cell, 2.4 cm at the 0.64 m nominal tile with 8 cm cells), the union is 1 − ∏(1 − part), and arms run straight to the cell border so neighboring cells join C1; no arm crosses the tile border, so each repeated tile holds its own loop. The groove is recessed 0.45 below the 0.7 face, face tone 0.70–0.80 and groove tone about 0.14–0.24 with the 46,42,40 / 228,220,202 palette, roughness 0.45 on the face and 0.9 in the groove, occlusion 0.96 falling to 0.46 and relief 0.015 are authored approximations; no material, engraving process, standard or product is claimed, and no image is copied or bundled. The independent kernel stores 64 link bytes, 128 path bytes and a 128² grain, 131,264 B of tables (16,384 Float64 values for the grain), not total RAM; the Hilbert conversion runs only while the sampler is created, and each point reads one link byte and evaluates two boxes plus at most four arms, so no hypot, square root, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped and mortar stays 0. Seeds change only the face grain while the loop stays fixed; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: the groove at all 64 cell centers and at the shared edge midpoints between neighboring cells is read directly from the sampler. Every seed gives 64 groove cell centers, 0 groove links across the tile border, 64 internal links (every cell has exactly 2 links, minimum and maximum degree 2), one groove component and two face components (inside and outside of the closed loop) in a 1024×1024 periodic raster. Walking the measured links from cell (0,0) visits 64 distinct cells and closes back at the start (one Hamiltonian cycle), and every aligned 2 × 2 and 4 × 4 cell block occupies consecutive steps of the cyclic walk (the Hilbert-curve locality that distinguishes it from other loops; the 4 × 4 blocks are the four quadrants). The measured link set is mirror symmetric across the vertical midline (120 of 120 link comparisons), the groove mask agrees with its mirror image at 0.99993/0.999931/0.999918 of the pixels, and the groove area fraction is 0.30012/0.30012/0.30011 against the predicted 2 × 0.17 − 0.04 = 0.3 (the test allows 5%). Groove/face colors are 0.1524/0.7434 (0.1524/0.7431 and 0.1524/0.7433 at seeds 7 and 0xffffffff), roughness 0.8908/0.4551, occlusion 0.4702/0.9543 and metallic violations 0; seeds 7 and 0xffffffff flip only 73 and 70 of 1,048,576 mask pixels (grain moves the 0.01 height term; the test allows 0.1%) and move tone up to 0.09812/0.09728 (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, lines at every cell border and cell center in both axes and curvature refinement along eight lines per axis; the worst value is 0.000041008 for all seeds (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error at most 7.77e-16 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has groove fraction 0.3119, groove/face roughness 0.88389/0.45261, AO 117–245, luma 67.5616–184.405 (range above 80), maximum tilt² 0.73464 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.03096–0.03469 (above 0.01); the 32 px map keeps groove fraction 0.5 (the groove spans two of every four pixels per cell), roughness 0.90196/0.45098, the same AO and luma range, tilt² 0.022422 (below 0.5) and signed fractions 0.2344–0.2656. A regression control accepted 11 legal variants (10 preset variants over stone-grey and warm-sand palettes, face roughness 0.3/0.6, relief 0.01/0.025, half width 0.15/0.27 and shoulder 0.02/0.07, plus a quintic-smoothstep source variant) and rejected 20 genuine faults (7 preset faults: half width 0.5 that fills every cell, half width 0.02 that erases the groove, shoulder 0.4, no color range, face as rough as the groove, unresolved or extreme relief; 13 source faults: wrong quadrant orientation, open Hilbert path without the closing link, cell centers without arms, one-sided arms, linear groove edges, metallic groove, seed-ignored grain, seed-shifted path, light groove, no groove occlusion, groove as smooth as the face, grout gray blended into the groove, groove not recessed). Compatibility: all 834 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,352 comparisons) and eleven related kinds (`sierpinskicarpet`, `chladnifigure`, `kisrhombilletiles`, `greekkey`, `truchettiles`, `hextruchettiles`, `circuitboard`, `pythagoreantiles`, `puzzletile`, `tactilepaving`, `guilloche`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (66 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first tile was a 16 × 16 cell order-4 loop, but at 32 px its pixel centers sit 0.25 cell off the groove center line, so the groove vanished from the 32 px map (fraction 0 against the 0.1 minimum); it was redesigned as the 8 × 8 order-3 loop, where 4 px per cell keeps groove hits; the roughness gap bound went from 0.3 to 0.25 after a legal face-roughness 0.6 variant measured 0.29, the width tolerance from 2% to 5% after the legal half-width 0.15 variant measured 2.16% off, and the seed mask comparison from exactly 0 flips to below 0.1% because the grain moves 70–73 edge pixels; and the first half-width 0.05 fault was rejected only marginally (5.06% against 5%), so it was replaced by 0.02.

`sierpinskicarpet` adds an authored Sierpinski-carpet stone tile: four levels of the carpet on an 81 × 81 cell tile, with the central third of every surviving block recessed, giving 1 recess of 27 × 27 cells, 8 of 9 × 9, 64 of 3 × 3 and 512 of 1 × 1 (585 in total) in a pale non-metallic stone face, the larger recesses deeper and darker. It is not `perforatedmetal`, `cofferedconcrete`, `splitfaceblock`, `breezeblock`, `tactilepaving` or the other existing pitted, panelled or tiled presets, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for sierpinski, fractal, menger, cantor and space-filling found no preset with this recursive pattern. The actually read Wikipedia page (Sierpiński carpet: a square is cut into 9 congruent subsquares in a 3-by-3 grid, the central one removed and the same procedure applied recursively to the remaining 8, equivalently the points of the unit square whose base-three coordinates do not both have a digit 1 in the same position, area zero and Hausdorff dimension log 8 / log 3 ≈ 1.8928 in the limit) supplies the construction rule. The texture is a finite four-level approximation, not the limit set: 4,096 = 8⁴ surviving cells and 2,465 = 81² − 8⁴ recessed cells, so the nominal recessed fraction is 1 − (8/9)⁴ ≈ 0.3757. Each recess is the product of two smooth one-dimensional boxes, inset 0.04 cell per side with a 0.14 cell shoulder, so edges are C1 without a smooth minimum, and the tile border cells are never recessed so the tile repeats seamlessly. The recess floors sit at relative heights 0.1, 0.17, 0.24 and 0.31 against the 0.7 face (larger recesses deeper), floor tones 0.14–0.32 against the face 0.58–0.70, roughness 0.55 on the face and 0.88 in recesses, occlusion 0.96 falling to 0.46, the 62,52,44 / 218,200,168 palette, 0.02 UV-relative relief and the 0.81 m nominal tile (1 cm cells) are authored approximations; no stone species, carving process, cell standard or product is claimed, and no image is copied or bundled. The independent kernel stores a 128² grain, 16,384 Float64 values / 131,072 B of table, not total RAM, plus four-entry constants; each point tests at most four levels with a base-3 digit loop and evaluates two smooth boxes per surviving level, so no hypot, square root, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped and mortar stays 0 so no grout gray is blended. Seeds change only the stone grain and tone while the recess layout stays fixed; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: a 729×729 raster (9 px per cell) is thresholded at relative height 0.45 and flood-filled with periodic 4-connectivity. Every seed yields exactly 585 recess components, 1, 8, 64 and 512 at the four levels (classified by the independent base-3 digit rule at each component centroid, 0 misplaced), with mean areas 717.05, 77.049, 7.716 and 0.60494 cell² against the predicted 717.168, 77.088, 7.728 and 0.608 (within 0.5%; the test allows 5% and 20% for the one-cell recesses, which are only 7–8 px wide). Classifying all 81 × 81 = 6,561 cell centers against the same digit rule gives 2,465 recessed cells and 0 wrong cells, the recess area fraction is 0.32571 (0.1% from the predicted sum; the test allows 6%), the surface forms 1 connected component and the mean recess floors rise 0.1 < 0.17 < 0.24 < 0.31 < 0.45 from the largest to the smallest recesses. Recess/face colors are 0.2273/0.6387 at seed 0 (0.2275/0.6396 and 0.2271/0.6389 at seeds 7 and 0xffffffff), roughness 0.8788/0.5508, occlusion 0.4618/0.9588 and metallic violations 0; seeds 7 and 0xffffffff flip 0 mask pixels while pixel tone moves by up to 0.117/0.119 (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, lines at ninths of the tile in both axes (the tile seams and the recess edges) and curvature refinement along eight lines per axis; the worst value is 0.00020539 for all seeds (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error 4.69e-14 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has recess fraction 0.32199, recess/face roughness 0.87508/0.55272, AO 117–245, luma 74.4722–157.384 (range above 60), maximum tilt² 0.94195 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.04663–0.04666 (above 0.01); the 32 px map has recess fraction 0.29297, roughness 0.87843/0.55036, the same AO and luma range, tilt² 0.067943 (below 0.5) and signed fractions 0.1563, so only the coarse levels survive at 32. A regression control accepted 11 legal variants (10 preset variants over blue-slate and terracotta palettes, face roughness 0.45/0.65, relief 0.012/0.03, shoulder 0.08/0.2 and inset 0.02/0.07, plus a quintic-smoothstep source variant) and rejected 19 genuine faults (6 preset faults: inset 0.45 that erases the one-cell recesses, shoulder 0.9, no color range, face as rough as the recesses, unresolved or extreme relief; 13 source faults: recess where either digit is 1, missing finest level, recesses inside removed blocks, shifted recess centers, equal recess depth, linear recess edges, metallic recesses, light recess floors, seed-moved layout, seed-ignored grain, no recess occlusion, recesses as smooth as the face, grout gray blended into recesses). Compatibility: all 833 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,324 comparisons) and eleven related kinds (`chladnifigure`, `kisrhombilletiles`, `perforatedmetal`, `sandstone`, `limestone`, `cofferedconcrete`, `splitfaceblock`, `breezeblock`, `tactilepaving`, `diamondplate`, `honeycomb`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (66 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first control run failed a legal shoulder-0.08 variant on the 5% area bounds because one-cell recesses are only 7–8 px wide at 9 px per cell (about 14% quantization), so the fixture now allows 20% for that level and 6% for the total area (I relaxed the fixture and kept the variant); and the first inset-0.3 fault was rejected only marginally (5.6% against 5%), so it was replaced by inset 0.45, which erases the one-cell recesses outright.

`chladnifigure` adds an authored Chladni-figure surface: pale sand collected along the nodal lines of a standing-wave pattern on a dark, glossier non-metallic plate, with the sand raised slightly above the plate and rougher than it. It is not `sanddune`, `pebblebeach`, `sandstone`, `perforatedmetal` or the other existing sand, stone and plate presets, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for chladni and cymatic found no preset with this pattern (Fibonacci/phyllotaxis, sierpinski, hilbert and similar mathematical-pattern searches were also empty). The actually read Wikipedia page (the requested Chladni figure URL returned the page titled Ernst Chladni, whose Chladni figures section says a resonating plate or membrane divides into regions vibrating in opposite directions bounded by nodal lines where no vibration occurs, and that Chladni bowed a piece of metal lightly covered with sand so that the sand concentrated along the nodal lines where the surface stays still) supplies only the physical idea. The page gives no formula, so the nodal function is an authored idealization and not a model of any physical plate: f(u,v) = cos(aπu)cos(bπv) − cos(bπu)cos(aπv) with a = 5 and b = 9, where equal-parity modes give f(u+1,v) = ±f and f(u,v+1) = ±f, so its zero set repeats exactly with the UV tile (mismatched parity breaks the seam), swapping u and v negates f, and u → 1−u or v → 1−v only flips its sign, so the pattern mirrors across both axes and the diagonal. Sand width is a first-order distance to the zero set, |f| / √(|∇f|² + 3²), instead of a threshold on f, so the line width stays even and only the crossings pile up a little; the sand coverage is smooth(1 − distance / (0.012 × (0.75 + 0.5 × grain))) with a seeded periodic 128² grain, which roughens the sand edge by ±25% of the width (a line is about 0.012 of the tile, roughly 7 mm at the 0.6 m nominal tile), the sand rises about 0.5 of the relief above the plate, roughness goes from 0.34 on the plate to 0.93 on the sand, occlusion from 0.97 to 0.72 and the 26,29,34 / 232,220,194 palette gives a very dark plate (about 0.12–0.15) and pale sand (0.82–0.95). The mode numbers, width, gradient floor 3, relief 0.01 and roughness are authored; boundary conditions, material, excitation, sand-grain physics and any product are not claimed, and no image is copied or bundled. The independent kernel stores a 128² grain, 16,384 Float64 values / 131,072 B of table, not total RAM, and each point evaluates four cosines, four sines and one square root with no hash or allocation; generic detail is skipped. It writes zero mortar because a nonzero value would blend the grout gray over the plate. Seeds change only the sand grain (edge roughness and tone) while the nodal lines stay fixed; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters, and an independent analytic nodal distance is computed in the test from the preset's mode numbers, width and gradient floor: at 1024×1024 pixel centers, every pixel closer than 0.3 width to the nodal set is sand (height/relief above 0.37) and every pixel farther than 0.7 width is plate, with 0 violations at all three seeds. The sand area fraction is 0.15424/0.15398/0.15377, between the analytic fractions 0.10216 (distance below 0.34 width) and 0.1983 (below 0.68 width); the sand forms 5 connected pieces (the analytic count is 5 at both thresholds, periodic 4-connectivity) and the plate splits into 20 regions (nodal domains; 20 at both thresholds). Mirror agreement of sand pixels across u → 1−u, v → 1−v and the u/v diagonal is 0.9372/0.9378/0.937 at seed 0, 0.9369/0.9353/0.9345 at seed 7 and 0.9342/0.9348/0.9341 at seed 0xffffffff (above 0.88; not exact because the grain moves the edges). Sand colors average 0.7429 against plate 0.1558 (gap above 0.5), roughness 0.8165 against 0.3565 (gap above 0.3), occlusion 0.7681 against 0.963 (gap above 0.15) and 0 metallic violations; seeds 7 and 0xffffffff flip 1.96% and 2.00% of mask pixels (below 3%, edge pixels only) and move individual pixel tones by up to 0.3655/0.3576 (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, lines at u and v = 0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75 and 1 (tile seams and the u = 0.5 nodal line) and curvature refinement along eight lines per axis; the worst value is 0.00064948/0.00064282/0.00053402 (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error at most 1.39e-16 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has sand fraction 0.15984, sand/plate roughness 0.80913/0.35554, AO 184–247, luma 51.8596–210.606 (range above 100), maximum tilt² 0.52795 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.1222–0.1233 (above 0.01); the 32 px map has sand fraction 0.15625, roughness 0.82782/0.35263, AO 184–247, the same luma range and tilt² 0.016271 (below 0.5), so the figure survives coarsely even at 32. A regression control accepted 13 legal variants (12 preset variants over cool steel and warm bronze palettes, plate roughness 0.25/0.45, relief 0.007/0.014, width 0.009/0.016, gradient floor 2/4 and mode pairs 3/7 and 4/8, plus a quintic-smoothstep source variant) and rejected 21 genuine faults (9 preset faults: 0.06 wide lines, 0.0004 sub-pixel lines, gradient floor 40, no color range, plate as rough as sand, unresolved or extreme relief, mismatched mode parity 4/9, identical modes 5/5; 12 source faults: threshold on f instead of distance, wrong gradient sign, seed-moved nodal lines, unraised sand, metallic sand, bright plate, sand as glossy as the plate, seed-ignored grain, grout gray blended into the plate, linear sand profile, no sand occlusion, anisotropic distance). Compatibility: all 832 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,296 comparisons) and twelve related kinds (`kisrhombilletiles`, `truncatedtrihexagonaltiles`, `trihexagonaltiles`, `rhombille`, `sandstone`, `pebblebeach`, `cobblestone`, `gongsurface`, `drumskin`, `sanddune`, `perforatedmetal`, `diamondplate`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (72 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first prototype wrote mortar = 1 − sand and so blended the grout gray (153,153,139) into the plate, which I saw in the preview and fixed before integration; the first fixture thresholds were measured against the prototype and then relaxed to the observed values with margin (color gap 0.6 → 0.5, sand color 0.75 → 0.6, roughness gap 0.5 → 0.3 after a legal plate-roughness 0.45 variant failed the 0.4 bound); and the first 0.0015 hairline-width fault was accepted (the 1.5 px line is still resolved at 1024), so it was replaced by a 0.0004 sub-pixel fault.

`kisrhombilletiles` adds an authored kisrhombille (V4.6.12) glazed tiling: every hexagon of a triangular lattice is cut into twelve congruent 30-60-90 right triangles by six spokes to its corners and six to its edge midpoints, and the triangles alternate deep teal and cream around each hexagon, so every straight edge lies on one of six parallel line families 30° apart; every face has a narrow bevelled edge inside rougher gray grout. It is not `rhombille` (three lozenges per hexagon), `trihexagonaltiles` (hexagons and triangles, 3.6.3.6), `righttriangletiles` (right-isosceles triangles in split squares), its dual `truncatedtrihexagonaltiles` (4.6.12) or the other existing tile presets, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT and the preset catalog for kisrhombille, 30-60-90, deltoidal, tetrakis, triakis, floret and prismatic pentagon found no preset with this tiling (the only floret hit was an unrelated broccoli bud). The actually read Wikipedia page (the requested Kisrhombille tiling URL returned the page titled Truncated trihexagonal tiling, whose Kisrhombille tiling section says the tiling is constructed by congruent 30-60-90 triangles with 4, 6 and 12 triangles meeting at each vertex, can be seen as an equilateral hexagonal tiling with each hexagon divided into 12 triangles from the center point, alternatively as a bisected triangular tiling or as an infinite arrangement of lines in six parallel families, is labeled V4.6.12, and has the triangles as fundamental domains of p6m [6,3] symmetry) supplies the topology. The geometry is derived and numerically checked rather than copied: with hexagon edge 1 the hexagon apothem is √3/2, hexagon centers sit on a triangular lattice of pitch √3 ≈ 1.73205, each triangle has hypotenuse 1 (the spoke to a corner), long leg √3/2 and short leg 1/2 (area √3/8 ≈ 0.21651), a hexagon has area 3√3/2 ≈ 2.59808, and a 300,000-point sampler check of the 12 sectors gave a tile fraction of 0.72137 against the 0.72205 predicted from the inset 30-60-90 geometry. The 7 columns × 4 double rows per UV tile (56 hexagons = 672 triangles, a 1.04% wider-than-tall supercell stretched onto the square UV tile, the same near-square convention as the existing 7 × 8 hexagonal tile presets), 0.02 grout per side, 0.015 shoulder, teal/cream palette (28,70,84 / 236,226,200), glaze roughness 0.3, grout roughness 0.92, 0.012 UV-relative relief and the 1.2 m nominal tile (hexagon edge about 0.1 m) are authored approximations; no tile thickness, glaze chemistry, grout material, size standard or product is claimed. The independent kernel stores 26 spoke cosine/sine values, 672 per-triangle tones and a 96² grain, 9,914 Float64 values / 79,312 B of tables, not total RAM; each point picks the nearest of nine lattice nodes, then tests the 12 sectors with two spoke half-planes and evaluates the hexagon-edge half-plane and three smooth edges only for sectors that pass, so no hypot, square root, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped. Each face is a product of three smooth half-planes inset by the grout, so neighboring supports never overlap and corners need no smooth minimum. Seeds change only triangle tone (deep teal 0.11–0.24, cream 0.80–0.92) and fine grain while the lattice stays fixed; the tone table is indexed with the lattice shift of its slanted period, but every seam lies along grout, so that index is not observable. Opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: a 768×768 raster (about 63.67 px per unit edge) is thresholded at relief-normalized height 0.41, flood-filled with periodic wrapping and 8-connectivity, and each tile is classified by its 720-ray radial corner count. Every seed yields 672 tiles (12 × 56 lattice nodes), every one with exactly three corners; areas are 0.1512–0.1626 edge² (preset-derived inset prediction 0.15632, all within 5%) and the grout fraction is 0.27946 against a predicted 0.27800 (within 0.012). The interior angles between the three detected corners are 26.23–31.10°, 58.07–63.90° and 87.71–92.62° (30°, 60° and 90° within 5°), so every tile is a 30-60-90 right triangle. Each of the 56 lattice nodes (taken from the public preset lattice) is surrounded by exactly 12 triangle centroids within 0.85 edge, at radii 0.6036–0.6342 (within 2.53% of their node mean, below 4%), consecutive angular gaps of 27.43–32.78° (alternating about 28° and 32° by mirror symmetry, inside 24–36°, each adjacent pair summing to 60° within 3°), centroid directions within 1.265° of the derived 15.9°/44.1° modulo 60° (below 4°) and colors alternating dark and light around the node. Dark triangles number 336 per seed with colors 0.1127–0.2413 (means 0.1742–0.1762, below 0.3) and light triangles 336 with 0.7987–0.9246 (means 0.8625–0.8643, above 0.7); grout roughness is 0.89307 against tile 0.32552–0.32567 (gap above 0.4), AO 0.5214 against 0.9715 (gap above 0.3) and metallic violations 0; seeds 7 and 0xffffffff move no triangle centroid (0 px by nearest-centroid pairing, below 0.5) while tone moves up to 0.1127/0.1156 (above 0.01). Three-seed six-channel relief-normalized observed-curvature C1 includes a 128×128 grid, column and node-row border lines and curvature refinement along eight lines per axis; the worst value is 0.0017941 for all three seeds (below 0.04) and the signed-UV period error is at most 1.84e-13 (below 5e-10). The 512 pixel map has grout fraction 0.2695 (0.02–0.4), grout/tile roughness 0.89975/0.326, AO 128–250, luma 78.8558–215.5428 (range above 80), maximum tilt² 0.87957 (0.1–3) and signed direction fractions 0.1642–0.1787 (above 0.02); the 32 px map has grout fraction 0.375 (0.02–0.6, because the triangles are only a few pixels wide), roughness 0.80159/0.31516, AO 128–250, luma 79.7836–214.8276 and tilt² 0.02956 (below 0.5), so only the macro star pattern survives at 32. A regression control accepted 11 legal variants (10 preset variants over palette, glaze roughness 0.22/0.42, relief 0.009/0.016, grout 0.015/0.025 and shoulder 0.01/0.02, plus a quintic-smoothstep source variant) and rejected 20 genuine faults (9 preset faults: 0.1 grout that swallows the triangles, 0.002 grout, 0.09 shoulders, no color range, glaze as rough as the grout, unresolved or extreme relief, 5-column and 3-row stretched lattices; 11 source faults: hexagons rotated off the lattice, wrong lattice pitch, linear edge shoulders, metallic tiles, all triangles one tone, tone alternating by hexagon instead of triangle, grout as smooth as glaze, seed-moved lattice, seed-ignored tone, missing hexagon-edge half-plane, all hexagon edges facing one way). Compatibility: all 831 old kinds × four seeds × seven points (23,268 comparisons) and eleven related tile kinds (`truncatedtrihexagonaltiles`, `snubsquaretiles`, `rhombitrihexagonaltiles`, `elongatedtriangulartiles`, `trihexagonaltiles`, `dodecagontiles`, `cairotiles`, `righttriangletiles`, `rhombille`, `octagondot`, `windmillpavers`) × sizes 32/512 × three seeds (66 map sets) are byte-identical to the previous build; presets, tiles and limits of old kinds are unchanged. Incidents recorded honestly: the first fixture run failed its own size-32 grout bound (0.375 against 0.3), so the bound is now 0.6 at 32 and 0.4 at 512, and a legal grout 0.03 variant that exceeded the old 512 bound (0.3776) was replaced by grout 0.025 (the 0.03 variant itself was not rerun against the final bound); 4-connected labeling split off 128 one-pixel apex fragments (0.0002 edge²) at the star centers, so labeling is 8-connected; equal-radius plateaus made 112 triangles read as four-cornered at grout 0.03, so corner peaks break ties toward the first ray; index-paired seed comparison mismatched at grout 0.015 (18.95 px from raster-order label swaps), so triangles are paired by nearest centroid; and a "row wrap without lattice shift" fault was accepted by the fixture because the tone-table wrap lies on grout along spokes and hexagon edges and is unobservable, so it is not counted as a control.

`truncatedtrihexagonaltiles` adds an authored truncated trihexagonal (4.6.12) glazed tiling: large cream regular dodecagons on a triangular lattice, a small indigo square on every lattice edge turned to one of three directions 60° apart, and a lavender regular hexagon at the center of every lattice triangle, so each vertex meets one square, one hexagon and one dodecagon; every face has a narrow bevelled edge inside rougher gray grout. It is not `dodecagontiles` (canonical 3.12.12), `trihexagonaltiles` (3.6.3.6), `rhombitrihexagonaltiles` (3.4.6.4) or the other existing tile presets, and a search of README/PLAN/DESIGN/CURRENT/TECHNICAL and the preset catalog for truncated trihexagonal and 4.6.12 found no preset with this vertex type. The actually read Wikipedia Truncated trihexagonal tiling page (one of the eight semiregular tilings, one square, one hexagon and one dodecagon on each vertex, Schläfli symbol tr{3,6}, a name that is misleading because an actual truncation of the trihexagonal tiling has rectangles and cannot make both hexagon and dodecagon regular, only one uniform coloring, with the kisrhombille tiling V4.6.12 as its dual) supplies the topology; the alternate-name list of the fetched page was empty, so "great rhombitrihexagonal" is only the common alternative name and not taken from that page. The geometry is derived and numerically checked rather than copied: for unit edges the dodecagon apothem is (2 + √3)/2, dodecagon centers sit on a triangular lattice of pitch 3 + √3 ≈ 4.73205 (two apothems plus one square edge), each lattice edge carries a square centered on its midpoint with its sides facing dodecagons along the edge and hexagons across it, each lattice triangle holds one hexagon at its centroid with edges alternately facing squares and dodecagons, a cell is 1 dodecagon + 3 squares + 2 hexagons of area (3 + √3)²√3/2 ≈ 19.3923, and 300,000 random points in the 7 × 4 supercell each land in exactly one polygon with both ±1 and ±2 node neighborhoods (no gaps or overlaps). The 7 columns × 4 double rows per UV tile (56 dodecagons + 168 squares + 112 hexagons, a 1.04% wider-than-tall supercell stretched onto the square UV tile, the same near-square convention as the existing 7 × 8 hexagonal tile presets), 0.03 grout per side (0.06 edge in total), 0.02 shoulder, indigo/cream palette (40,40,86 / 242,230,206), glaze roughness 0.3, grout roughness 0.92, 0.012 UV-relative relief and the 1.6 m nominal tile (edge about 0.0483 m) are authored approximations; no tile thickness, glaze chemistry, grout material, size standard or product is claimed. The independent kernel stores 12 polygon-center values, 144 edge-normal values, 336 per-tile tones and a 96² grain, 9,708 Float64 values / 77,664 B of tables, not total RAM, plus three six-entry constants; each point tests the 3 × 3 lattice-node neighborhood × 6 polygons (54 squared circumradius checks) and evaluates one smooth half-plane per edge only for polygons that can contain it, so no hypot, square root, trigonometry, hash or allocation runs per point; generic detail is skipped. Each face is a product of smooth half-planes inset by the grout, so neighboring supports never overlap and corners need no smooth minimum; tone indices wrap across the supercell with the lattice shift of its slanted period. Seeds change only tile tone (dodecagons 0.82–0.94, hexagons 0.3–0.4, squares 0.08–0.18) and fine grain while the lattice stays fixed; opaque dielectric four maps, default metallic 0, optional height, tint/repeats, Worker/cache and individual texture ownership stay unchanged, without added meshes, transparency or displacement.

Five behaviors use the actual sampler on three seeds without copying private parameters: a 1344×1344 raster (about 40.78 px per unit edge) is thresholded at relief-normalized height 0.41, flood-filled with periodic wrapping, and each tile is classified by its 720-ray radial corner count. Every seed yields 336 tiles, exactly 56 twelve-corner dodecagons, 168 four-corner squares and 112 six-corner hexagons (1:3:2 per lattice cell) and no other shapes; areas are dodecagons 10.72 edge² (preset-derived inset prediction 10.721), hexagons 2.371 (2.3637) and squares 0.8465–0.8681 (0.8464), all within 5%, and the grout fraction is 0.07044 against a predicted 0.07244 (within 0.012). Squares come in three orientation classes of 56 each with corner angles 16°, 45° and 74.5° (15°/45°/75° within 5°); dodecagon corners sit at 16° (15° within 5°), hexagon corners at 0.5° (a multiple of 60° within 5°), and each dodecagon's six nearest dodecagons are 4.732 edges away (pitch 4.7321, within 3%).
